**No way to act on a listed orphaned account.** Investigation showed this wasn't just a missing button — there was no *endpoint* that could reach one. `deactivate_org_user`/`archive_org_user` (`routers/orgs.py`) both require the target to already belong to the acting admin's organisation (a SOC 2 hardening fix from an earlier pass) — which an orphaned user, by definition, never does. So the exact accounts this review exists to surface were the one category of user nobody in the system could ever deactivate. Two new system-level endpoints close this, deliberately scoped to only ever touch org-less accounts (`_require_orphaned_user`, 400 otherwise — an org member must still be managed through their own organisation's admin console, keeping a server admin's authority tenancy-wide but content-free per I-M-05):
- `POST /api/v1/system/users/{id}/deactivate` (C-U-04; blocks self-targeting)
- `POST /api/v1/system/users/{id}/reactivate` — a genuinely new capability: no lifecycle action anywhere in this codebase previously reversed a deactivation at all, org-scoped or otherwise.

**Grant/revoke server admin got a UI, and a safety net it didn't have before.** `PUT /api/v1/system/users/{id}/server-admin` (I-M-06) already existed but had no frontend surface at all. Building one surfaced a real, previously-unexploited gap: nothing stopped a server admin from revoking their *own* flag (or another's) down to zero active server admins deployment-wide — an unrecoverable lockout, since nobody would then hold the authority to grant it back to anyone short of direct database access. Fixed by rejecting a revocation that would leave zero active server admins (counting only active ones, since a deactivated admin can't do anything anyway) — the same "last admin" shape as `leave_organization`'s existing sole-org-admin check, just computed by count rather than self-targeting alone (a server admin can revoke *someone else's* flag too, not just their own).

**Frontend**: `ServerAccessReviewPage` gained a "Show" filter (Orphaned accounts / Server administrators / All users — the last needed to find a user to grant server admin to in the first place, since no user-search UI exists anywhere else in the app) and an "Include deactivated accounts" checkbox, both independent axes. Per-row actions adapt to what's actually valid for that user: Deactivate/Reactivate for org-less accounts, Grant/Revoke server admin for everyone. `SystemUserOut` gained `is_server_admin` and `has_org_membership` (a plain boolean, not naming any organisation — no confidentiality regression, since `no_org_membership=true` already let a caller infer this one user at a time).

New regression tests in `test_access_review.py`: the orphaned filter excludes a server admin while the `is_server_admin` filter finds them; `has_org_membership` reflects reality; deactivate/reactivate an orphaned account (proven via an actual login attempt before/after, not just the flag); an org member is rejected (400) at both new endpoints; self-targeting is rejected; revoking the deployment's last active server admin is rejected, while revoking one of two succeeds. Manually verified end-to-end in a real browser (login, switch views, deactivate/reactivate, grant/revoke, and the last-admin-revoke error message rendering), per this project's UI-testing convention — including confirming the safety net actually blocks the browser action, not just the API call directly.

**Verified for real**: full backend suite: 234 passed (228 + 6 new), zero regressions. Full Playwright suite re-run once on freshly reseeded data: 16 passed. Manual browser verification of every new interaction described above.

## UI: persisted view mode, collapsible nav rail and sections, theme-aware chrome

Four related UI requests: the tile/list view toggle should be a real user preference (not per-browser localStorage); the left nav should collapse to icons; card sections (the user's concrete example: "Create" inside the Personal Access Tokens card) should collapse to just their title; and the nav rail should follow the active theme instead of always being dark.

**A general-purpose `ui_preferences` key/value bag, not a one-off field.** The first cut added a narrowly-typed `view_mode_preferences: dict[str, str]` column. Prompted by the user to generalise it before building more UI on top, since a nav-rail-collapsed flag and per-section collapse state were about to need the exact same shape. Renamed to `User.ui_preferences: dict[str, Any]` (JSONB, mirroring `Organization.sso_group_mappings`'s "structured but open-ended" precedent) with no per-value schema validation — any low-stakes display preference is just a new namespaced key (`view_mode:<page>`, `nav_rail_collapsed`, `section_collapsed:<key>`), never a migration. `PATCH /auth/me/preferences`'s `ui_preferences` field is shallow-merged into the existing bag by top-level key (reassigned, not mutated in place, so SQLAlchemy's change-tracking sees it), so setting one key never clobbers another. A new frontend hook, `useUiPreference(key, default)`, wraps this generically (reads `useAuth()`'s `user.ui_preferences`, writes via a new `setUiPreference` on `AuthContext` that updates local state optimistically and PATCHes in the background) — `useViewMode`, the nav-rail collapse toggle, and `CollapsibleSection` are all now thin, one-line specialisations of the same hook, not three separate implementations.

**Tile/list view mode** (`ViewToggle.tsx`'s `useViewMode`): previously read/wrote `localStorage`, so a user's choice didn't follow them across browsers/devices, unlike every other preference on this page (theme, landing page, digest mode). Now backed by `useUiPreference` under the key `view_mode:<pageKey>` — the same three call sites (`projects`, `requirements`, `change-requests`) needed no change beyond the hook's own implementation.

**Collapsible nav rail** (`Layout.tsx`): a new chevron button at the foot of the rail toggles `railCollapsed` (`nav_rail_collapsed` preference), independent of the pre-existing `menuOpen` mobile show/hide toggle. Every nav label is now wrapped in `<span className="nav-label">`, hidden via a `.nav-rail-icons` CSS rule when collapsed, so collapsing is a pure CSS/class change rather than conditionally rendering different JSX per link.

**Collapsible sections** (`components/CollapsibleSection.tsx`, new): a header button + chevron wrapping arbitrary children, collapsed state persisted per `sectionKey` via `useUiPreference`. `variant="card"` renders a full `.card` (top-level sections: Profile, Security, Personal Access Tokens, Notification preferences); `variant="plain"` renders just the header with no background (nested subsections: Change password, Two-factor authentication, and the PAT card's Create form — the user's named example). Applied throughout `PreferencesPage.tsx`. One real regression caught by the existing Playwright suite: the "Change password" section header and the "Change password" submit button inside it ended up with the same accessible name, breaking `pelion-v2.spec.ts`'s `getByRole('button', {name: 'Change password', exact: true})` locator (`strict mode violation`, two matching elements). Fixed generally, not just for this one case: every `CollapsibleSection` header gets `aria-label="<title> section"` when its title is a plain string, disambiguating it from any same-named control in its own content — a legitimate a11y improvement independent of the test collision it happened to also fix.

**Theme-aware nav rail** (`theme.css`): the nav rail previously used a dedicated, always-dark `--color-nav-*` variable set, explicitly documented as "NOT swapped per data-theme" (matching an earlier reference-mockup decision). Removed entirely — the rail now uses the same `--color-surface`/`--color-border`/`--color-text`/`--color-primary` variables as every other panel, so it follows the active light/dark theme like the rest of the UI, per the user's explicit ask.

**Verified for real**: new backend test (`test_ui_preferences_are_shallow_merged_not_replaced`) proves the merge-by-key semantics, including that setting an existing key overwrites only that key and that values persist across requests. Full backend suite: 235 passed (234 + 1 new), zero regressions. Full Playwright suite re-run once on freshly reseeded data, including the fix for the `pelion-v2.spec.ts` collision: 16 passed. Manually verified in a real browser: tile/list choice survives a full page reload (proving server-side persistence, not just component state); nav rail collapses to icons and back, and that state also survives a reload; the "Create" PAT subsection collapses to just its title; switching the theme to dark re-colours the nav rail along with the rest of the page.

### Follow-up: hamburger button, wasted width, and section-label-as-divider

Immediate feedback after the above landed: the header's hamburger (☰) "did nothing," `PreferencesPage` wasted most of the screen (a hardcoded `maxWidth: 480` on its root `<div>` — pre-existing, not introduced by the work above, just never noticed until the cards next to it started using full width elsewhere), and the request to make the collapse "via an icon" with the section labels ("Server Management") "turn to a line" so the icon rows don't visibly jump between expanded/collapsed states.

- **Hamburger did nothing because it never controlled `railCollapsed`** — it only toggled a separate `menuOpen` boolean that hid the whole rail below a 720px media query, which has no effect at desktop widths. Rather than run two overlapping collapse mechanisms, `menuOpen` was removed outright and the hamburger now toggles the same `railCollapsed` preference as the chevron at the foot of the rail — one collapse behaviour, two equivalent triggers, both meaningful at any screen width.
- **`PreferencesPage`'s `maxWidth: 480` removed** — every other admin-style page (`OrgAdminPage`, `ProjectAdminPage`, `RequirementsPage`) uses the full container width with no cap; Preferences now matches that convention instead of being a one-off exception.
- **Collapsed section labels now render as a thin divider line** (`text-indent: -9999px` off-screen + a 1px `background: var(--color-border)` bar) instead of `display: none`, so collapsing keeps a comparable amount of vertical space where "Project"/"Global"/"Server Management" used to be, rather than the icon rows abruptly shifting upward. The bottom chevron toggle lost its text label to become purely iconic, consistent with the header icon buttons (Preferences/Sign out) that were already icon-only with a `title` tooltip.

**Verified for real**: `npm run build`/`lint` clean; full backend suite (235 passed) and full Playwright suite (16 passed) re-run on a freshly reseeded stack — two failures seen mid-session (`workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`'s favourite-button locator) turned out to be the already-documented test-data-pollution artifact from repeated manual/automated runs against the same live stack without an intervening reseed, not real regressions — confirmed clean on this final from-scratch cycle. Manually re-verified in-browser: hamburger and foot-of-rail chevron both collapse/expand the same rail; Preferences cards now fill the available width; collapsed section labels render as visible divider lines.

## Bug: OrgAdminPage hung forever for a server admin (or anyone) with no role in that org

The user reported a specific org admin URL stuck on "Loading…" forever. Root cause: `OrgAdminPage.reload()` bundles five API calls (`org`, `groups`, `resources`, `projects`, `report-templates`) in one `Promise.all` with no error handling at all, called from a `useEffect` with no `.catch()`. Every one of `groups`/`resources`/`report-templates` requires at least `member` in that specific organisation; a caller with no role there (a server admin browsing the org list they can always see, or a stale bookmark) gets a 403 on all three, the `Promise.all` rejects, `org` state is never set, and the page's only loading gate (`if (!org) return <Spinner />`) spins indefinitely with nothing but a silent unhandled-rejection in the console.

**First fix**: wrapped the bundle in try/catch, added a `loadError` state, and rendered the actual error message (`ApiError.message`, e.g. "Insufficient organisation permissions.") with a link back to Organisations instead of hanging. This alone would have been a regression for server admins, though: I-M-05's one documented carve-out is that a server admin can create an organisation and its initial user with zero prior role — but the *only* UI for that ("Organisation users → New user") lived inside the very page this fix now blocks outright for exactly that caller.

**Investigating that surfaced a real, separate gap**: the user asked how a server admin is supposed to manage/set up an org's users at all if they can't access it. Tracing the actual authorization rules: `create_org_user` (`POST /orgs/{id}/users`) already allows `require_org_admin_or_server_admin` — creating a *new* native user works fine with zero prior role. But `assign_org_role` (the only way to grant a role to an *existing* user) requires the caller to already be `org_admin` of that org — a chicken-and-egg that meant a server admin had no way to make *themselves* admin of an org they created, only to bootstrap someone else's account. This matters for self-hosting deployments specifically, where the server admin is the only person running the system and wants to use their own org, not just provision customers' orgs (a hosting-company shape the existing carve-out already covered fine).

**New endpoint**: `POST /orgs/{id}/join-as-admin` (`routers/orgs.py`) — server-admin-only, self-targeting only, always grants exactly `org_admin`, 400 if already an admin. Deliberately not a general "server admin can grant any role to any user in any org" capability (see access-control-policy.md's amended item 1) — it only lets the platform's single most-trusted actor take on ordinary membership in one specific org, for themselves, mirroring the one-time `server_admin_create_org` bootstrap behaviour (`services/bootstrap.py`) as a general, repeatable, in-app action instead.

**`OrgAdminPage` degraded view**: when the initial bundle 403s, a server admin now sees the org's name (fetched standalone — `GET /orgs/{id}` already has its own server-admin bypass, which is exactly why this one call would have succeeded inside a bundle that otherwise failed) plus two actions: "Become admin of this organisation" (calls `join-as-admin`, then reloads — succeeds fully since they're now a genuine member) and "Create its first admin user" (a minimal email/name/password form posting to the existing `create_org_user` with `role: "org_admin"`, for the hosting-company case where the server admin bootstraps someone *else's* account and deliberately doesn't become a member themselves). A non-server-admin hitting the same 403 still sees the plain error page — they have no carve-out to offer.

New regression tests in `test_audit_fixes.py`: joining as admin works and immediately unlocks ordinary org-admin actions; joining twice is rejected (400); a non-server-admin (even one who's an admin elsewhere) is rejected (403). Manually verified both flows end-to-end in a real browser: created two fresh orgs, used "Become admin" on one (landed on the full, normal admin page afterward) and "Create its first admin user" on the other (confirmed via a direct login as the newly-created account that it can actually administer that org).

**Verified for real**: full backend suite: 238 passed (235 + 3 new), zero regressions. Full Playwright suite re-run once on freshly reseeded data: 16 passed. `docs/soc2/policies/access-control-policy.md` amended (Authorization item 1) to document both server-admin carve-outs precisely, since this is exactly the kind of authorization-surface change CLAUDE.md requires flagging.

## Organisation disable and hard delete

The user asked for a way to handle an organisation that's stopped paying for hosting or simply needs to be archived — offered the choice of a reversible disable, an irreversible hard delete, or both; the user chose both, in the same pass.

- **Disable is a pure access gate, not a data-affecting operation.** `Organization.is_active`/`disabled_at`/`disabled_by` are the only new columns; nothing else changes when an org is disabled. A new `_require_org_active(db, organization_id)` helper in `services/rbac.py` is called — after PAT-scope checks, before the role check — from every org/project-scoped dependency factory (`require_org_role`, `require_org_admin_or_server_admin`, `require_project_role`, `require_project_view`, `require_project_manage`), raising 403 "This organisation has been disabled." **This deliberately blocks the org's own admins too**, not just outsiders — a disable that the org's own admin could route around wouldn't be a usable "stop this org" control at all. It is deliberately *not* applied to `require_server_admin`, `join_organization_as_admin`, or the disable/enable/delete endpoints themselves, since the server admin must always be able to toggle an org's state regardless of its current state, and login itself is untouched (a disabled org doesn't lock out a user who belongs to other, active orgs).
- **Delete is irreversible and gated behind typing the organisation's exact current name** (`OrganizationDeleteConfirm.confirm_name`, checked server-side in `DELETE /orgs/{id}`, not just a client-side guard) — the same "type the name to confirm" pattern used by GitHub/GitLab repo deletion, appropriate given there is no undo.
- **Cascade strategy: DB-native `ON DELETE` actions on the FK columns, not ORM `cascade=` relationships.** Nearly every cross-reference in this codebase is a plain FK column queried via `select()`, not a declared SQLAlchemy `relationship()` collection, so `ondelete="CASCADE"`/`ondelete="SET NULL"` on the column definitions themselves (added across `models/{organization,project,requirement,change_request,custom_field,notification,audit,engagement}.py`) is the mechanism that actually matches how the code reads data, rather than adding a parallel ORM-level cascade layer that would only fire on ORM-mediated deletes. Audit trail rows use `SET NULL` (not `CASCADE`) on `AuditEvent.organization_id`/`project_id` specifically — the audit *row* survives (its `entity_type`/`entity_id`/`detail` already capture the substance of what happened, and the row itself has ongoing compliance/incident-investigation value), only the FK link to the now-gone org is nulled.
- **Three things a DB cascade cannot clean up, handled explicitly in `services/org_deletion.py::delete_organization_cascade`**: (1) real object-storage bytes — a DB-only cascade would delete `FileAsset` rows but leave orphaned bytes in S3/local storage, which `data-retention-and-disposal-policy.md` already treats as a disposal failure, so every file asset is routed through the existing `delete_file()` (removes bytes *and* row) before the org row itself is deleted; (2) `PersonalAccessToken.allowed_organization_ids`, a JSONB list rather than a real FK — every PAT scoped to the deleted org has that org's id stripped from its list (mirroring the existing `descope_org_pat` logic exactly), auto-revoking the token if the list becomes empty; (3) two genuinely polymorphic tables with no real FK at all (`ReviewComment.target_id`/`target_type`, `Subscription.entity_id`/`entity_type`) — a DB cascade would silently orphan these rather than error, so they're deleted explicitly by id, scoped to the org's requirement/change-request ids, before the cascade runs.
- **Users are never deleted, only their `UserOrgRole` rows** (removed via cascade) — a user who loses their only org membership this way becomes "orphaned," a case the access-review tooling already covers (see the "Orphaned-account review" section above).
- **`ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx`** gained a Status column and per-row Disable/Enable/Delete actions (`window.confirm` for disable, since it's reversible; an inline card requiring the exact name for delete, matching the backend's own check so the button is genuinely disabled — not just hidden — until it matches). **`OrgAdminPage.tsx`**'s existing degraded-view logic (from the section above) gained a third branch: when the 403 message indicates the org is disabled, a server admin sees the org's name plus an "Enable this organisation" action instead of the join/create-admin actions, which only make sense for "not a member," not "disabled."
- **Fixed a now-stale assertion while doing this**: `server-admin-scope.spec.ts` previously asserted a server admin's degraded view showed *zero* instances of the org's name — true before the join-as-admin degraded view existed, false now that showing the org name is precisely how that view (and this new disabled-org view) lets the admin confirm which org they're looking at before acting. Updated to assert the name renders as a heading while `Users`/other content still does not.

**Verified for real**: 10 new backend tests (`test_org_lifecycle.py`), including one, `test_delete_cascades_through_everything_including_non_fk_references`, that creates one of literally everything an org can own (project, requirement, comment+reaction, subscription, change request, an uploaded file with real storage bytes, a solo-scoped PAT, a second two-org PAT) and asserts each category is actually gone — not merely orphaned — after deletion, while confirming the surviving member's user row, the two-org PAT (now scoped to just the remaining org), and the audit trail (rows survive, `organization_id` now null, plus exactly one new `action="deleted"` event) all behave exactly as designed. Full backend suite green, zero regressions. New `server-org-lifecycle.spec.ts` Playwright spec drives the full create → disable → re-enable → delete-with-confirmation flow through the real UI; full Playwright suite re-run clean on freshly reseeded data. Manually re-verified the same flow once more directly in a browser against the live dev stack (create "Manual Test Org," disable, confirm "Disabled" badge, enable, confirm "Active" badge, delete with a wrong name first to confirm the button stays disabled, then the exact name, confirm the row disappears).

`docs/soc2/policies/data-retention-and-disposal-policy.md`'s Known Gaps item 1 updated to reflect that hard deletion is no longer an unbuilt gap for organisations specifically (still true for individual users/projects in isolation, which remain deactivation/archival-only).

### Hardening-pass follow-up

Per CLAUDE.md's requirement to run the identify → verify → remediate review for security-sensitive changes, a dedicated hardening pass was run against this feature after it shipped: two parallel candidate-identification sub-agents (backend RBAC/cascade-delete logic; frontend/API surface), then a fresh, independent verification sub-agent per candidate finding — the same process used by this codebase's earlier hardening passes (see "Security hardening pass" above). All five candidates were confirmed real; none were false positives.

**Fixed:**

- **WebSocket connections fully bypassed the org-disable gate** (`routers/ws.py`). `project_updates` authorizes inline (its own `UserProjectRole`/`ProjectGroupMember` query) rather than through one of the five `require_*` dependency factories, so it was never wired to `_require_org_active` at all — not just the documented "already-open socket keeps streaming" exception, but a full bypass: a member could open a **brand-new** connection to a disabled org's project and stream indefinitely. Fixed by resolving the project's organisation id at handshake and rejecting (code 4403) if it's disabled, and by extending the existing periodic re-check (`_user_session_still_valid`, already used for `token_version`/`is_active` revocation) to also re-check `Organization.is_active`, so an already-open connection now closes within one `_EXPIRY_CHECK_INTERVAL_SECONDS` interval too — matching the account-deactivation guarantee this same function already provided. A new boolean-returning `rbac.is_org_active` was added alongside the existing exception-raising `_require_org_active`, since a WebSocket handler can't propagate an `HTTPException` and needs to translate the check into its own close code instead.
- **File downloads fully bypassed the org-disable gate** (`routers/files.py`). `download_file` authorizes via the raw `get_effective_org_roles`/`get_effective_project_roles` helper functions rather than a `require_*` dependency (it needs `?token=` query-param support for `<img src>` use, which those don't offer), so — like the WebSocket case — it was never wired to the check at all. A disabled org's shared-resource files and requirement attachments both remained fully downloadable by any member who still held a role row. Fixed by calling `_require_org_active` explicitly on both code paths, resolving the organisation id via the file's own `organization_id` (org-resource case) or via the new `_project_organization_id` helper (requirement-attachment case), matching the pattern `require_project_view` already uses.
- **Hard-deleting an organisation that had ever had a logo or SSO login-background image set crashed outright, and destroyed other files' storage bytes in the process.** `Organization.logo_file_id`/`login_background_file_id` are self-referential FKs to `file_assets` with no `ondelete` action (an oversight in the original FK-coverage sweep — every *other* org/project-scoped FK got one). `delete_organization_cascade`'s file-deletion loop calls `delete_file()` (which deletes storage bytes before the DB row) on every `FileAsset` the org owns, including its own logo/background asset, with nothing nulling those two columns first — deleting that one `FileAsset` row raised a raw, unhandled `IntegrityError` (no handler exists in `main.py`, so this surfaced as a 500), discovered only via SQLAlchemy's autoflush partway through the loop, by which point every file processed *before* the crash had already had its storage bytes irreversibly deleted while its DB row (and the org's) survived the rolled-back transaction — orphaned metadata pointing at bytes that no longer exist, for an org that wasn't even successfully deleted. Verified via a live repro against the test database before fixing. Fixed with a single targeted Core-level `UPDATE` nulling both columns before the file-deletion loop runs, so the FK reference is gone before any `FileAsset` row is touched — this removes the crash and, since the loop can no longer fail partway through, the storage-data-loss blast radius along with it.
- **`GET /me/reviews/due` leaked requirement metadata (unique code, name, review date) from a disabled organisation indefinitely.** This is the one org-content read in the app with no single `project_id`/`organization_id` path parameter to hang a `require_*` dependency off (it deliberately spans every project the caller has a role in), so it had never been wired to any org-scoping check at all, let alone `_require_org_active`. Fixed by joining `Project`/`Organization` into the existing query and filtering `Organization.is_active`. `get_due_reviews_for_project` (the project-scoped sibling, used by `routers/requirements.py`'s `GET .../reviews/due`) was already covered for free via its `require_project_view` dependency and needed no change.
- **An organisation created with a blank or whitespace-only name defeated `DELETE /orgs/{id}`'s "type the exact name to confirm" safety gate.** `OrganizationCreate.name` had no length or blank validation; for an org named `""`, both the frontend's `deleteConfirmText !== org.name` check and the backend's own `confirm_name != org.name` check are trivially satisfied by an empty string, so the "must not be a stray click" guarantee that gate exists for silently didn't hold for such an org. Fixed with a `field_validator` on `OrganizationCreate.name` that strips surrounding whitespace and rejects a blank result (422), applied only at creation — there is no separate org-rename endpoint. Requires an already-server-admin actor to trigger in the first place (org creation is server-admin-gated), so this was a self-inflicted footgun rather than a privilege-escalation path, but still a genuine defeat of a documented safety control.

**Verified for real**: 7 new backend regression tests (2 in `test_websocket_security.py` covering both the handshake rejection and the periodic-recheck path; 5 in `test_org_lifecycle.py` covering both file-download code paths, the logo/login-background delete-crash fix — reproducing the exact scenario that used to 500 — the reviews-due leak, and blank/whitespace-name rejection including that meaningful surrounding whitespace is still trimmed, not rejected). Full backend suite: 255 passed (248 + 7), zero regressions. Three pre-existing tests in `test_ws_active_recheck.py` updated for `_user_session_still_valid`'s new `organization_id` parameter — no behavioural change for those, since they pass `None`.

## UI/UX audit: dark header chrome, light-theme contrast, responsive nav rail, and a couple of workflow dead ends

A Playwright-driven UI/UX review (enterprise/business-app lens, Power Apps as the named reference) found that the header rendered with the same `.card`/`--color-surface` styling as every other panel — no distinct chrome at all — on top of weak light-theme contrast, a real horizontal-overflow bug below ~860px, and a couple of dead ends in the requirement-creation flow. The user asked to implement the full list; fixes below, roughly in review order.

**Header chrome is theme-independent** (`theme.css`, `Layout.tsx`): a new token set — `--color-header-bg` (`#14161c`), `--color-header-text` (white), `--color-header-text-muted`, `--color-header-hover-bg`, `--color-header-active-bg`, `--color-header-border` — is declared at the top-level `:root`, outside the `[data-theme="light"|"dark"]` blocks, so the top bar stays a solid black bar with bold white text in both themes rather than a themed surface, matching the Power Apps/Dynamics/ServiceNow convention the user asked for. `.app-header` replaces the header's previous `.card` styling; `.app-header .btn`/`.text-muted` need their own override, since the base `.btn` styling (drawn from `--color-surface`/`-border`/`-text`) would otherwise render as a jarring white chip (light theme) or a near-invisible ~1.1:1 ghost (dark theme) against a black bar. Icons (`lucide-react`, `currentColor`) and the wordmark's inline colour follow automatically.

**Light-theme contrast** (`theme.css`): `--color-bg` moved from `#f7f8fa` to `#eef1f5` and `--color-border` from `#d8dce1` to `#c3c9d1`, taking the measured page-background-vs-card-surface contrast up from 1.06:1 and giving card/rail boundaries an actually-visible line; `--shadow-sm` deepened slightly (0.08 → 0.12 alpha) for the same reason. Dark theme was left alone — its accent colours already cleared 4.5:1+.

**Nav rail now auto-collapses below ~860px, closing a real overflow bug**: at a 600px viewport, `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` measured 712px before this fix — the existing 720px breakpoint only ever touched tables/`.grid-metrics`, never the rail's fixed 220px width. `.nav-rail`'s `min-width` moved out of an inline style (previously driven only by the `railCollapsed` JS boolean) into CSS classes (`.nav-rail` / `.nav-rail-icons`), so a new `@media (max-width: 860px)` block can force the rail into its existing icon-only layout regardless of the user's manual expand/collapse preference, reusing the icon-only rules rather than adding a second collapse mechanism. The four pages using an inline `1fr 240px` two-column layout (`RequirementsPage`, `ChangeRequestsPage`, `RequirementDetailPage`, `ChangeRequestDetailPage`) now share one `.side-grid` class, which collapses to one column in the same breakpoint.

**Reviewer assignment replaced a raw-UUID text box with an org-member picker** (`RequirementDetailPage.tsx`): the field previously asked for a "User ID" typed by hand. Checking for a picker pattern to reuse turned up that this is the *only* way the app assigns a user to anything anywhere — the group-membership picker in `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` does the same raw-ID-in-a-text-box thing — so this fix is scoped to the one field actually in the review rather than a wider component extraction. The page now fetches the project's `organization_id`, then `GET /orgs/{id}/users` (the same org-member-directory endpoint `OrgAdminPage` already uses, open to any org role per its own docstring) and renders a `<select>` of "display name (email)" options; if that call fails (e.g. the viewer has no org role), it falls back to the original plain text input rather than breaking the page. If the currently-assigned reviewer isn't in the fetched list (e.g. a deactivated account), an extra option preserves their id with a "no longer a member" hint instead of silently dropping the selection. The read-only review-section badge on the same page now resolves the id to a display name too.

**Requirement-creation dead end on brand-new projects, fixed** (`RequirementsPage.tsx`): a project with no components/categories yet rendered "New Requirement"'s Component/Category `<select>`s with zero options and a permanently-disabled Create button, no explanation offered. The form now checks for that case first and shows a notice with a direct link to Project Admin instead. The two selects also gained visible `<label>`s (previously unnamed in the accessibility tree, unlike every other field on the same form).

**Smaller fixes**: two nav icons that were reused for different destinations (`CalendarClock` for both "Requirements due for review" and "My reviews due"; `ShieldCheck` for both "Organisations" and "Access review" under Server Management) now use distinct icons (`Clock`, `UserCog`), so the collapsed icon-only rail is unambiguous. The Requirements list table's Name column got an explicit width hint (was getting comparable space to Component/Category despite carrying the longest text). `RequirementsPage`/`ChangeRequestsPage` gained the same `{list.length === 0 && <empty-state message>}` pattern already used on Projects/History/My-Reviews, instead of silently rendering an empty table with just headers.

**One flagged finding turned out to be a false positive on closer inspection, and was deliberately not "fixed"**: the review claimed the OIDC client secret field on Org Admin returns the real plaintext value into the DOM. Tracing it end-to-end: `OrgSsoConfigOut` (both the `GET` and `PUT` response schema in `routers/orgs.py`) has no `oidc_client_secret` field at all, the column is encrypted at rest (`EncryptedString`), `main.py`'s validation-error redaction list already covers the field name, and an existing test (`test_oidc_provisioning.py`) already asserts `"oidc_client_secret" not in body`. The frontend field starts blank and only sends a value when the admin types a new one (blank means "leave unchanged"). The review agent almost certainly read back a value it had itself just typed into the field while exploring the form, not something the server returned — a case of the codebase's own instruction to challenge another agent's assumptions rather than implement a "fix" for correct, already-tested behaviour.

**Verified for real**: `tsc -b`/`eslint` clean on every changed file. Frontend container rebuilt and every fix re-checked against the live stack with Playwright: header renders solid black with bold white text in both themes; light-theme card/rail borders are now visibly distinct from the page background; at a 600px viewport `scrollWidth` now equals `innerWidth` (600, was 712) with the rail auto-collapsed to icons; the reviewer `<select>` on a real requirement is populated with the actual org member ("Server Administrator (admin@example.com)") instead of a text box; a freshly-created project with no components/categories shows the dead-end notice and its Project Admin link instead of a disabled form. (One environment quirk hit mid-verification, not a code issue: the Playwright browser's HTTP cache kept serving a stale, differently-hashed CSS/JS bundle from before the frontend container was rebuilt; resolved by forcing a fresh navigation. Worth remembering if a future container rebuild "doesn't seem to apply" in a live Playwright check.)

## UI/UX follow-up: pinned full-height nav rail, content-width preference, and inline component/category creation

Immediate follow-up to the pass above, driven by three specific asks plus two smaller things noticed while reviewing the live app again post-header-fix: remove the header's ☰ button (confirmed pure duplicate of the rail's own chevron toggle — same `railCollapsed` state, no other consumer), make the nav rail full-height and flush against the true left edge instead of a short, inset floating card, and let a project manager/administrator create a missing component/category directly from the "New Requirement" empty-state instead of being sent to Project Admin. Two open questions (rail scroll behaviour; what happens to main content once the rail is flush-left) were put to the user rather than guessed at, given the shell-restructuring extent of the second item — answered: pin the rail while scrolling, and default main content to full-bleed with a **user-facing preference** to opt back into the previous capped width, rather than a fixed developer choice either way.

- **Burger button deleted outright** (`Layout.tsx`) — no replacement; the chevron at the foot of the rail is the sole collapse/expand control now, at every screen width.
- **Nav rail is now `position: fixed`, not sticky-in-flow** (`theme.css`): `top: var(--header-height)` (a new `56px` constant, alongside a matching fixed `height` on `.app-header` so the rail's offset has something stable to key off), `left: 0`, `bottom: 0` — flush against the true viewport edge regardless of scroll position, and full height below the header rather than a short card sized to its own content. Its width moved from an inline style driven only by the `railCollapsed` boolean into two CSS custom properties (`--nav-rail-width: 220px`, `--nav-rail-width-collapsed: 56px`), which are now the single source of truth for both the rail's own width and the offset applied to content next to it.
- **Main content is pushed clear of the now-out-of-flow rail via a matching `margin-left`**, expressed as a general-sibling CSS rule (`.nav-rail ~ .app-content`, and a `.nav-rail-icons` variant) rather than inline JS-computed margin — keeps the width source of truth in one place and transitions smoothly alongside the rail's own collapse animation. The existing 860px "force icon-only regardless of manual state" media query got a matching `~ .app-content` override so the two stay in sync at narrow widths too.
- **Content-width is a new persisted display preference** (`content_boxed` in the existing generic `ui_preferences` bag — same mechanism as `nav_rail_collapsed`, not a new dedicated `User` column), exposed as a "Content width" select in Preferences right next to Theme. Default is full-bleed (`Layout.tsx` renders `<main className="app-content">` with no inner wrapper); switching to "Boxed" adds a `.boxed` class plus a `.content-inner` child capped at the same `1200px` the whole app used everywhere before this pass, so nothing about table/form readability regresses for anyone who explicitly wants the old width back.
- **A pre-existing, previously-invisible bug surfaced immediately during verification**: `App.tsx` had its own `<div className="container" style={{ padding: "1.5rem 0" }}>` wrapping every route's content, independent of `Layout.tsx`. This was harmless before — it happened to cap at the same 1200px the old rail-in-container layout already capped at — but once `.app-content` was made deliberately full-bleed-by-default, this second, un-toggleable 1200px cap silently overrode the new preference entirely (both "Full width" and "Boxed" measured identically at 1200px on a 1600px viewport until this was found and removed). Deleted; `Layout.tsx`'s own `.app-content`/`.content-inner` is now the only place page width is decided.
- **Component/category quick-create moved inline** (`RequirementsPage.tsx`): the empty-state notice added in the previous pass (component/category dead end) now branches on a new `canManageProject` check — `myRoles` (via the existing `useMyProjectRoles` hook) including `project_manager`/`project_administrator`, **or** the viewer being an org admin of the project's organisation, which `myRoles` alone can't see (it's a per-project role list; org-admin-implies-project-manage is a separate rule the backend's `require_project_manage` already enforces). Resolved the same way the reviewer picker in the prior pass did: fetch the project for its `organization_id`, then `GET /orgs/{id}/users`, and check the caller's own entry for the `org_admin` role — reusing an existing endpoint rather than adding a new one. When `canManageProject` is true, the notice grows one small name+prefix form per missing item (component and/or category), mirroring `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`'s own form exactly (same strings, same layout) and posting to the same existing endpoints; a successful create reloads the page's data, which — since `newComponentId`/`newCategoryId` auto-select the first available option — transitions straight into the full "New Requirement" form with the new component/category already selected, no extra step. Stakeholders (who can create requirements but, per the stricter backend gate, not components/categories) still see the original "go to Project Admin" link — never a control that would 403 on click.
- **One item from the prior pass's report corrected rather than acted on**: "Stage progress repeats the stage name" turned out to be a demo-data coincidence, not a real duplication — `s.name` (the stage's own name) and `STAGE_STATUS_LABEL[s.status]` (its lifecycle status badge) are genuinely different fields that only rendered identically because the specific demo stage happened to be named "Scoping" while also *in* "scoping" status. No code change made.

**Verified for real**: `tsc -b`/`eslint` clean across every changed file. Frontend container rebuilt twice (once before the `App.tsx` wrapper was found, once after) and re-checked against the live stack with Playwright: the rail measures `position: fixed`, `left: 0`, `top: 56`, `height: 844` at a 900px-tall viewport (exactly filling the remainder below the header); a 600px-wide viewport still shows zero horizontal overflow with the rail auto-collapsed; the content-width toggle was driven through the real Preferences UI both directions and measured a real `.side-grid` width difference (1332px full vs. 1200px boxed at 1600px wide) only *after* the `App.tsx` fix — before that fix both settings measured identically, which is how the bug was caught rather than assumed away. The inline creation flow was driven end-to-end against a freshly-created, component-and-category-less project as the org-admin account: filled and submitted both mini-forms, watched the notice hand off into the real "New Requirement" form with "Backend Services (SVC)" / "Performance (PERF)" pre-selected. Scratch project archived afterward.

## UI/UX review #3 implemented: nav icon, org/platform branding, activity labels, a Style Manual casing pass, report fixes, tooltips, and a help page

A third review pass raised seven items; three carried a genuine open question each, put to the user rather than guessed at, before implementing all seven. Decisions: (1) the new graphite accent colour becomes the one default everywhere, including the report generator's previously-separate defaults; (2) header branding fallback is resolved by sole-org-membership — a viewer in exactly one organisation sees that org's branding even on pages with no single project context, otherwise the platform default applies; (3) the help page is built in-app, content-managed through plain Markdown files rather than JSX.

**Nav rail expand icon** (`Layout.tsx`): `ChevronLeft`/`ChevronRight` (an ambiguous bare arrow) replaced with `lucide-react`'s `PanelLeftClose`/`PanelLeftOpen` — icons purpose-built for a sidebar-panel toggle.

**Org and platform-wide branding** — a real, two-layer feature, not a token tweak:
- `Organization` gained `accent_color_hex` and `header_title` (both nullable — null means "use the platform default"). A new `ServerSettings` model (`services/branding.py::get_server_settings`, a lazily-created singleton row, no DB-level singleton constraint) holds the platform default `accent_color_hex`, `default_logo_file_id`, `default_header_title`. Pre-release convention (see "Schema migration strategy" above) applied: both land as plain model fields, not a hand-written incremental migration; the already-running test stack's database was patched directly via `ALTER TABLE`/`CREATE TABLE` matching what `create_all()` would generate, rather than reset, to preserve its seeded demo data mid-session.
- **One colour is stored, not two.** `contrast_text_hex` (Python, `services/branding.py`) and its TS mirror (`utils/color.ts`) pick black-or-white button text by relative luminance; `ensureContrast` (TS only) derives a lightened variant for dark theme and a darkened variant for light theme from the *same* stored hex on demand, rather than asking an admin to pick two colours. Platform default: `#475569` (light) / auto-lightened for dark — checked at 7.6:1 / 6.9:1 against white/dark text respectively, both clearing WCAG AA with room to spare. `theme.css`'s own light/dark `--color-primary` fallbacks were updated to match, for the instant before `BrandingContext`'s fetch resolves.
- **`BrandingContext.tsx`** (new) resolves `{ logoFileId, headerTitle, accentColorHex }` and applies the colour live via `document.documentElement.style.setProperty`, re-run whenever the resolved light/dark theme changes (not just when the colour does) so the derived tint stays correct across a theme toggle. Resolution: inside a project's URL space, that project's organisation (falling back field-by-field to the platform default); everywhere else, the viewer's sole organisation if they belong to exactly one, else the platform default — the exact three-way split above. This supersedes `TerminologyContext`'s old `orgLogoFileId` (removed; logo/title/colour are now one concern with one set of resolution rules, terminology overrides are a separate, narrower one).
- **The platform default logo reuses the existing avatar/org-logo access bypass** (`routers/files.py`) rather than requiring `FileAsset.organization_id` to become nullable: a platform-wide asset is still stored against *an* organisation (whichever exists first — cosmetic, storage-key-only) but served to any authenticated user via the same carve-out avatars and org logos already use, extended by one more `ServerSettings.default_logo_file_id` check.
- **UI**: `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s logo card became a "Branding" card (logo + header title + an "override the platform default" checkbox gating a colour picker); a new `ServerSettingsPage.tsx` (`/server/settings`, server-admin only for writes, its `GET` open to any authenticated user since the app shell needs it for everyone) provides the platform-wide equivalent. `ReportTemplate.accent_color_hex`'s DB default and the plain/unbranded PDF path's hard-coded table-header colour (previously `#2563eb` and `#2d3748`, two more uncoordinated defaults) both moved to the same `#475569`, per the "unify everywhere" decision — a plain report now visually matches the app it came from.

**Activity feed — labels and an entity-type filter**: tracing every renderer of `ChangeEntry` found three genuinely different implementations (the project overview card and history page rendered raw `entity_type`/`action` with no actor; `ActivityPanel`'s sidebar list showed the actor but never the entity type, through an incomplete, mostly-identity `ACTION_LABEL`). Consolidated: `ChangeEntry` now imported from the one definition in `api/types.ts` (was duplicated locally, missing `actor_display_name`, in two of the three files) everywhere; new `ENTITY_TYPE_LABEL`/`activityActionLabel`/`describeActivityEntry` (`api/types.ts`) give one consistent "{badge: entity type} {actor} {action}{detail suffix}" rendering used by all three surfaces. `GET /projects/{id}/changes` gained an `entity_type` query param (`services/changes.py::get_project_changes`, filtered post-merge across the three/four underlying sources rather than pushed into each query — none are large enough per-project for that to matter, and two mint their `entity_type` in Python, not from a column); `ProjectHistoryPage.tsx` got a matching filter dropdown.

**Text casing, against the Australian Government Style Manual's "minimal capitalisation" rule** (sentence case: capitalise only the first word and proper nouns) — traced to one root cause: only `StageStatus` had a display-label map anywhere in the app; every other enum rendered its raw snake_case wire value straight into badges, dropdown options, and (via `services/reports.py`) generated PDF/CSV output. New maps in `api/types.ts` — `REQUIREMENT_STATUS_LABEL`, `REQUIREMENT_LEVEL_LABEL`, `CHANGE_REQUEST_STATUS_LABEL`, `PROJECT_ROLE_LABEL`, `ORG_ROLE_LABEL`, `CUSTOM_FIELD_ENTITY_KIND_LABEL`, `CUSTOM_FIELD_TYPE_LABEL` — applied at every raw-render site found (`RequirementsPage`/`RequirementDetailPage`/`ChangeRequestsPage`/`ChangeRequestDetailPage`/`ProjectAdminPage`/`OrgAdminPage`/`ProjectListPage`/`ReportsPage`'s status dropdown), plus a Python-side `services/labels.py::requirement_status_label` for the two report formats. A handful of hand-typed Title Case strings were fixed alongside (`nav.changeRequests`, `nav.admin`, `orgAdmin.backToOrganizations`, the requirements table's "Target · Level"/"Component · Category" headers). The bigger, non-obvious fix was `TerminologyContext.tsx`: its resolved terms (`useTerm`) are substituted mid-phrase ("New requirement") as often as used standalone (a heading, a nav label) — `DEFAULT_TERMS` moved to lower case and `useTermPlural` now capitalises itself, since *every* current plural call site is a standalone heading/nav label and *no* singular call site is, which resolved both without needing a second hook variant. One casing bug was caught only during live verification, not by the static sweep: the project overview's "Requirements by status" donut built its legend from `Object.entries(metrics.requirements_by_status)` directly, bypassing every badge-based fix — the one `Object.entries`/`Object.keys` render site that fed raw keys straight to a chart instead of a `<span className="badge">`.

**Report output**: the PDF table's ID column (e.g. `AUTH-SEC-001`) was wrapping mid-code at 2.2cm; widened to 3cm, taken from the Reasoning column (the widest, least space-constrained), so the table's total width is unchanged. Downloaded filenames now match what the backend already intended: the CSV endpoint gained the same `{project name}-requirements.csv` `Content-Disposition` the PDF endpoint already sent (a new `_filename_safe()` strips characters unsafe in a header), and the frontend's blob-download `<a download>` attribute — which wins over the header in most browsers — now builds the same project-qualified name instead of a hard-coded `requirements.pdf`/`.csv`. `ReportTemplate`'s four configurable fields beyond `name` (accent colour, cover page, include-logo, footer text) were fully wired into the API already but had zero UI beyond the name-only create form; `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s create form now sends and exposes all four.

**Tooltips and a help page**: new `components/Tooltip.tsx` — hover/focus-triggered, no delay, replacing reliance on the native `title` attribute (slow, OS-dependent, absent on touch) for icon-only controls. Applied across `Layout.tsx` (every nav-rail link plus the header's notification/preferences/sign-out buttons and the collapse toggle), `NotificationBell.tsx`, and paired with `aria-label` (previously missing on most of these — the project list's favourite-star button was the sole existing example of the correct `title`+`aria-label` pairing) on the same controls plus `RequirementsPage.tsx`'s move-up/down buttons and `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s display-name lock toggle. A new `/help` route (`HelpPage.tsx`, linked from the nav rail's Global section) renders five Markdown files (`frontend/src/help/*.md`: how the app is organised, the requirement lifecycle, change requests, roles, reports) through a new, deliberately minimal `utils/markdown.ts` renderer — headings/paragraphs/bullet-lists/bold/italic/code/links, mirroring the exact subset `services/reports.py`'s own Markdown-to-flowable renderer already supports, chosen over adding a Markdown dependency to keep this frontend's very small dependency footprint (four runtime packages before this) unchanged. Updating the help content going forward is editing or adding a `.md` file — no JSX or build-step changes needed beyond one import line and one `SECTIONS` entry for a genuinely new file.

**Help moved to the header, diagrams, screen-aware tooltips (real fix, not the first attempt), Markdown-or-WYSIWYG report content with org defaults, and a CSV import column-mapping wizard** — four separate user requests in one round:

- **Help in the header, with diagrams**: the `/help` link moved from the nav rail's Global section into the header's icon cluster (`Layout.tsx`, next to Preferences) — a global "how does this app work" reference belongs with the other account-level chrome, not competing with per-project navigation. `utils/markdown.ts`'s renderer gained fenced-code-block support, with a special case for ` ```mermaid ` blocks (rendered as `<div class="mermaid">{raw content}</div>`, unescaped, since Mermaid parses its own source; any other language renders as an escaped `<pre><code>`). `HelpPage.tsx` now calls `mermaid.run()` on mount, themed light/dark off `data-theme`. Two new diagrams (`02-requirement-lifecycle.md`, `03-change-requests.md`) show the full requirement and change-request state machines, each with the explanatory paragraph immediately before it that CLAUDE.md's documentation rules require. `mermaid` is a new runtime dependency — justified because the user named it directly ("eg mermaid diagrams"), unlike the custom-built renderer/editor work below.
- **Tooltip screen-awareness — the clipping was never a placement-math bug.** The first fix in this round only corrected *where* the bubble's CSS classes pointed (centre/left/right, above/below), computed once on mount and re-measured only on `window.resize`. Live-verifying it against the actual bug report (collapsed nav rail) showed it still failed: collapsing the rail is a layout change that fires no `resize` event at all, so the placement went stale the moment the rail collapsed after page load — and worse, once wrong, a subsequent resize would re-measure the bubble's *already-shifted* position rather than its true default, compounding the drift. Root cause once that was fixed and it *still* clipped: the nav rail's `overflow-y: auto` implicitly computes `overflow-x: auto` too (a CSS Overflow spec rule — you cannot have one axis clip and the other stay `visible`), so any absolutely-positioned descendant extending past the rail's collapsed ~52px width was clipped by the rail itself, no matter how correct its left/right math was. Fixed properly: `Tooltip.tsx` now renders the bubble through `createPortal(..., document.body)` as a viewport-`fixed` element with plain pixel `top`/`left` computed from the trigger's `getBoundingClientRect()` plus the bubble's own intrinsic (placement-independent) width/height, re-measured on every show (not just on mount/resize) — this escapes *any* scrollable/clipping ancestor generically, not just the nav rail one bug report happened to name. Recorded here in detail because it's a two-layer bug (stale measurement, then ancestor clipping) that a plausible-looking first fix did not catch — only live verification with Playwright, not `tsc`/`eslint`, surfaced it.
- **Report intro/chapters/appendices: Markdown or WYSIWYG, saved per project, with organisation defaults.** New `components/RichTextEditor.tsx` — a Markdown/Rich-text mode toggle over either a plain `<textarea>` or a `contentEditable` div driven by `document.execCommand` (deliberately not a TipTap/Slate/Quill dependency, matching this codebase's minimal-dependency philosophy elsewhere). Switching *into* rich mode renders the current Markdown via `renderMarkdown()`; switching *out* serializes the DOM back via a new `htmlToMarkdown()` (`utils/markdown.ts`) — imperative, one-way sync in each direction, never a controlled re-sync from the `value` prop while the user is actively typing, which is what avoids the classic contentEditable caret-reset bug. Backend: `Organization` gained `default_report_intro`/`default_report_chapters`/`default_report_appendices` (same pre-release "plain model fields, `ALTER TABLE` the running test DB directly" convention as the branding fields above); `services/reports.py::resolve_report_config` resolves each of the three fields independently via `project.field or org.default_field or ""` — the same truthy-OR-fallback shape as branding, chosen for the same reason (avoids a nullable-column migration on `Project`, at the deliberate cost of not distinguishing "genuinely blank" from "not customised"). New `GET`/`PUT /orgs/{id}/report-defaults` (org-admin gated, same pattern as existing report-templates endpoints — no SOC 2 policy implications, since nothing here touches auth/RBAC/secrets/multi-tenant isolation). `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`'s Report Setup tab now shows "(organisation default)" wherever a field is falling back, using three new `*_is_organisation_default` response flags; the chapter-list editor (title + move-up/down/delete + body) was extracted from `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` into a shared `components/ReportChapterListEditor.tsx` so `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s new "Report Defaults" card could reuse it rather than duplicate it.
- **CSV import: verified working, then given column mapping and a requirements legend.** The import endpoint (`POST /projects/{id}/requirements/import`, `routers/requirements.py`) already had real test coverage (`test_requirement_lifecycle.py::test_import_creates_valid_rows_and_reports_errors_for_invalid_ones`) and was re-verified live via Playwright end-to-end this round — it was not previously broken. What was missing was answers to the user's other two questions: the file had to already use the backend's exact column names, and nothing in the UI said what those names or which fields were required. New `components/CsvImportWizard.tsx` parses the chosen file client-side with Papaparse (a new runtime dependency — chosen over hand-rolling RFC4180 parsing, judged too risky for a feature whose whole point is data-integrity-sensitive field mapping), auto-guesses a mapping from the file's own headers to the six canonical fields by exact/normalised name match, and shows an editable mapping table (with a `*` marker and one-line explanation per field) plus a live preview of the first five rows under the current mapping — the "Map Name, Component, Category before importing" line and a disabled Import button block submission until every required field is mapped. On confirm, the file is remapped client-side into a canonically-headed CSV (via `Papa.unparse`, keyed by field name into an object rather than a positional array — the first version of the template-download builder used a positional array and silently shifted `reasoning`/`component_prefix`/`category_prefix` by one column relative to its own header row; caught only by downloading and diffing the actual bytes in Playwright, not by `tsc`) and posted through the existing, unchanged import endpoint. A "Download template" button generates a one-row example CSV pre-filled with the current project's own first component/category prefix and stage name, so a new user has a concrete, valid starting point rather than a blank header row.

Verified for real, not just by `tsc`/`eslint`: full backend pytest suite (256 passed after adding `test_project_falls_back_to_organisation_report_defaults` and updating one pre-existing assertion in `test_reports.py` for the new response fields) against the already-running `tests/container` stack, patched live via `ALTER TABLE` for the two new `Organization` columns; both frontend containers rebuilt and Playwright-driven through every surface touched — the Help link's new header location and both Mermaid diagrams actually rendering; the tooltip fix specifically re-tested against the exact reported scenario (collapsed nav rail) after the first fix attempt was shown to still fail live; the Markdown↔rich-text round trip on a real edit; the org-default fallback appearing on a second project that had never set its own report content, then getting overridden once that project set its own; and the CSV wizard's full mapping-to-import path with a deliberately mismatched-header file, ending in a real "2 created, 0 error(s)" and the new requirements appearing in the list.

**Verified for real**: full backend suite — 255 passed, zero regressions — run inside the actual test stack container (`python3 -m pytest`; the bare `pytest` script doesn't get the cwd-on-`sys.path` behaviour `-m` invocation does, which is what made `import app.models` fail at first). Per the documented "pytest wipes the shared test database" gotcha, the stack was restarted and reseeded afterward with `scripts/seed_demo_data.py`'s realistic multi-project dataset rather than the minimal manual fixtures, which turned out to matter: it's what surfaced the donut-chart casing miss above, real actor names in the activity feed, and a real `ReportTemplate` to generate an actually-branded PDF from. `tsc -b`/`eslint`/`vite build` clean throughout. Live-verified with Playwright across three accounts (a fresh single-org admin, a multi-project org admin, the platform server admin): the graphite accent colour rendering on buttons/active-nav-state/links in place of the old blue; the org Branding card and the new platform Branding page both saving and reflecting correctly; a real branded PDF generated via the API showing "Approved"/"Completed" (not raw lowercase), every ID on one line, and the project-qualified filename in its `Content-Disposition` header; the activity feed showing real names ("Jordan Alvarez created", "Sam Whitfield voted") with properly-labelled entity badges; the help page's five sections rendering with correct heading/list/bold formatting; and a tooltip bubble appearing immediately on hovering a collapsed nav-rail icon.

**A batch of thirteen nav/IA and admin-page requests in one round** — the largest single-turn UI change this project has had, so recorded item by item:

- **Tooltips only while the nav rail is collapsed.** `Layout.tsx`'s per-row rendering was pulled into a new `NavRailLink` that only wraps the link in `<Tooltip>` when `railCollapsed` is true; expanded, the link's own `nav-label` text already says the same thing a tooltip would, so wrapping it there was pure noise.
- **Server Management consolidated, section renamed.** "Access review" (`ServerAccessReviewPage.tsx`) and "Platform branding" (`ServerSettingsPage.tsx`) — two standalone nav entries — were deleted and merged into one new tabbed `ServerManagementPage.tsx` (`/server/management`, replacing both old routes) with the exact two-tab layout `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` already established. The nav-rail section they lived in, previously "Server management", is now "Administration", alongside the unchanged `/server/organisations` link (its own row-per-org table with create/disable/delete doesn't fit a tab).
- **"Organisations" (`/orgs`, `OrgListPage.tsx`) → "Org Management", with a single-org skip.** Belonging to exactly one organisation no longer shows a one-item list to click through — `orgs.length === 1` now renders `<Navigate to={/orgs/{id}/admin} replace />` directly. Its own nav-rail entry (Global section) was removed entirely rather than renamed: for a server admin it was a strict subset of the Administration section's `/server/organisations` list (same `GET /orgs` call, confirmed by that page's own docstring), and non-admin reachability was preserved differently — see the Preferences "Your access" tab below, which links to `/orgs/{id}/admin` directly per org rather than through this intermediate list.
- **OrgAdminPage.tsx sections collapsed by default, Users first.** Every top-level card became `<CollapsibleSection defaultCollapsed>` (the component already existed, used elsewhere on `PreferencesPage.tsx`, just not here) and the "Organisation users" section was reordered to the top of the page — it's the section an admin reaches for most.
- **Notification popup**: `NotificationBell.tsx` now closes on every route change (`useEffect` keyed on `location.pathname`, since the header bell is rendered outside the routed content and nothing was ever telling it to close) rather than staying open until manually toggled. The empty-state's apparent excess padding was really "Mark all read" showing with nothing to mark plus the same spacing as a populated list" — the button now only renders when `unreadCount > 0`, and the empty state's own padding/gap is tightened specifically for that case.
- **Org filter on the Projects page, "Organisations" removed from Global nav.** New `organization_id` query param on `GET /projects` (`routers/projects.py::list_projects`) — done server-side rather than client-side filtering the already-paginated list, since client-side filtering after pagination would silently show fewer results than actually match. `ProjectListPage.tsx` gained a matching `<select>` in its filter row, shown only when `orgs.length > 1` (same gating the existing new-project org picker already uses).
- **Nav-rail collapse toggle moved to top-right.** Was a full-width bar at the foot of the rail; now a plain `.btn` icon button in its own right-aligned row at the very top, above the section links — reachable without scrolling past a long project section first. The existing `PanelLeftOpen`/`PanelLeftClose` icon-per-state logic was already semantically correct on inspection (open-icon shown while collapsed, close-icon while expanded) and re-verified visually via Playwright screenshot at both states rather than changed blind.
- **Preferences page: tabbed, plus a new "Your access" tab.** The four existing top-level `CollapsibleSection`s (Profile, Security, Personal Access Tokens, Notification preferences) became tabs in the same button-row pattern as `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`, keeping their *nested* `CollapsibleSection`s (Change password, Two-factor, PAT creation) as-is — those are genuinely subsections, not separate concerns. The new "Your access" tab lists every org the user belongs to with their role badges there, a "Manage organisation" link (shown only when `org_admin` is among those roles) straight to `/orgs/{id}/admin`, and that org's projects with per-project roles — resolved by calling `GET /orgs/{id}/users` once per org and finding the caller's own entry (no endpoint returns "my role in every org I'm in" directly; fine in practice since a user belongs to a handful of orgs at most, not hundreds) plus the project list's existing `my_roles`/`organization_id` fields, no new backend endpoint needed.
- **Header: no more separate Settings icon.** The `<Settings>` icon-button was deleted; the username text itself is now the link to `/preferences`, with a `title`+`aria-label` pair (the Tooltip-only label would have collided with e2e specs' pre-existing `getByTitle("Preferences")` locators — see below).
- **Nav-rail links are full-width, not shrink-wrapped.** `.nav-link` gained `width: 100%; box-sizing: border-box`, and a new `.tooltip-trigger` class (Tooltip's own wrapper span, previously an unclassed inline style) gets `.nav-rail .tooltip-trigger { display: flex; width: 100% }` — both the click target and the `.active` background now span the rail's full width in both collapsed and expanded states, not just the icon+text's own content width.
- **A Favourites nav entry and page**, shown only when the user actually has a favourited active project (`Layout.tsx` checks this via a small `GET /projects?archived=false` on mount and again on arrival at `/projects` or `/favourites` specifically — not on every navigation, which would otherwise fire that same request on every route change for the entire session). New `FavouritesPage.tsx` (`/favourites`) reuses the same tile-card rendering as the projects list, filtered to `is_favorite`.
- **Terminology split into its own `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` tab**, out of "Project settings" — `saveSettings` split into itself (name/summary/flags) and a new `saveTerminology`, each with its own button, since they're saved independently now.
- **Project groups tab shows actual members.** `member_user_ids` was always just a list of UUIDs with a bare count; the tab now fetches the org's user directory once (`GET /orgs/{id}/users`, same "any org role can call this unfiltered" endpoint as the Preferences access tab) to resolve each id to an email/display name, lists them with a remove button (wired to the existing, previously-unused-from-the-UI `DELETE .../groups/{id}/members/{id}`), and replaced the raw-UUID "User ID" text input with a `<select>` of the org's users excluding whoever's already a member — matching this codebase's established "replace raw UUID input with a real picker" precedent from an earlier round's reviewer-picker fix.

**Two real bugs found only by running the actual existing Playwright suite, not just live-clicking the changed surfaces**: (1) several `e2e-workflows` specs and `pelion-v2.spec.ts`/`golden-path.spec.ts` located "Change Requests"/"Project Admin"/"submitted"/"approved" via `exact: true` locators against Title-Case/lowercase text that the earlier text-casing-pass round (see above) had already changed to sentence case ("Change requests", "Project admin", "Submitted", "Approved") — a latent gap from that round that this round's fixes to the underlying markup didn't cause but did make newly reachable; fixed by matching the specs' locators to the current casing rather than reverting the app's casing. (2) `getByTitle("Preferences")`/`getByTitle("Notifications")` in `pelion-v2.spec.ts`/`golden-path.spec.ts` depended on a native `title` attribute that the `Tooltip.tsx` rollout (a *previous* round this session) had quietly stopped setting on those two controls specifically — restored (`title={label}` alongside `aria-label`, per `Tooltip.tsx`'s own docstring suggestion) rather than changing the tests, since a redundant `title` is harmless and other Tooltip-wrapped controls elsewhere were never relied on this way.

**Verified for real**: backend `python3 -m pytest` — 256 passed — after adding the `organization_id` project-list filter; `tsc -b`/`eslint` clean across every touched file. Both containers rebuilt; every one of the thirteen items live-verified via Playwright screenshots/DOM inspection (not just accessibility-tree snapshots) as a demo org admin, a platform server admin, and a fresh single-org account — including confirming a tooltip actually renders (opacity/visibility inspected directly on the portaled DOM node, since a screenshot taken immediately after a synthetic hover can miss it) and that the org-role-scoped "Manage organisation" link and group-member add/remove round-trip against real data. The full existing Playwright suite (17 spec files) was then run repeatedly to catch regressions from the nav restructuring — surfacing the two pre-existing gaps above, both fixed — plus a design smell of its own: the Favourites-nav-link check originally re-ran on *every* navigation; tightened to mount-time plus arrival at `/projects`/`/favourites` only, the two places a favourite can actually change. Later full-suite runs against the same long-lived (10+ hour) container session intermittently showed 10+ unrelated failures, traced to environmental exhaustion (a container restart lost the E2E dataset entirely; personas 401'd) rather than any code issue — resolved by reseeding and confirmed with a final clean 17/17 pass.

## A 28-item batch across security, notifications, admin, and the component/category data model

The largest single-turn request this project has had — a full backlog dump rather than a themed review — worked straight through in one pass (the user's explicit choice, over item-by-item confirmation, given the scale) with check-ins reserved for genuinely security-sensitive or ambiguous decisions. Four such questions came up and were put to the user rather than guessed at; each is called out below where it lands. The CLAUDE.md instructions were themselves extended first, at the user's request, with a `## Testing Requirements` section (every UI feature needs a Playwright test; every backend change needs a test pinning it to the request) and a `## Playwright MCP Usage` section (prefer the spec suite; reserve the live-browser MCP tools for targeted one-off investigation) — both binding on this round's own work and cited below where they shaped the approach.

**Org-wide two-factor requirement.** The request's own wording was truncated mid-sentence ("An org a..."); resolved via clarifying question as "org admins can require 2FA org-wide," not a platform-wide switch. `Organization.require_2fa` (new boolean, default `False`) is enforced in `services/rbac.py::_require_org_2fa`, checked alongside the existing `_require_org_active` at all five RBAC dependency call sites (`require_org_role`, `require_org_admin_or_server_admin`, `require_project_role`, `require_project_view`, `require_project_manage`) rather than at login — a user who hasn't enabled 2FA yet can still authenticate and see enough of the app to go turn it on, but every org-scoped action inside a 2FA-required org is blocked until they do. `PreferencesPage.tsx` exposes the org's own toggle as a `ToggleSwitch` (new pill-switch component), not a button, per the same clarifying answer.

**Notifications became a first-class surface, not just a dropdown.** New `NotificationsPage.tsx` (`/notifications`, linked from the nav rail's Global section) gives a searchable, paginated history of everything the bell only ever showed a truncated recent slice of. The bell's own dropdown gained a "Mark all read" button (only rendered when there's an unread count to clear, matching the empty-state padding fix from an earlier round) that clears every active notification in one call rather than requiring one click per item.

**Requirement/change-request status badges became click-to-filter.** New `FilterBadge` component (a `.badge`-styled `<button>` with an `active` prop for visual affordance) replaces plain status badges on `RequirementsPage.tsx` (status) and `ChangeRequestsPage.tsx` (status and target stage); clicking a badge sets that filter, clicking the same one again clears it — the same list-filtering state that already existed via dropdowns, now reachable directly from the data itself. `ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx` and `ProjectListPage.tsx` got the same treatment for an Active/Disabled organisation filter (see below).

**Personal access tokens gained project scoping.** `PersonalAccessToken.allowed_project_ids` (JSONB list, default empty = "no extra restriction beyond org scope" — an empty list was chosen over a nullable column so "not yet scoped" and "explicitly scoped to nothing" can't be confused) is validated at creation (`routers/pats.py::create_pat`) against the projects the *creating user* can actually reach, and enforced per-request via new `services/rbac.py::check_pat_project_scope`. A new `GET /pats/max-lifetime` endpoint feeds `PreferencesPage.tsx`'s expiry picker so it can auto-fill and warn ("expiry too long") against whatever ceiling the server actually enforces, instead of a value hard-coded on the frontend that could silently drift from the backend's real limit. The creation form's project list uses a checkbox list, not the new `UserAutocomplete` (that component is for picking one person from a directory; a PAT's scope is a multi-select over a much shorter, already-visible list).

**Banning a user — scoped to orphaned and not-yet-onboarded accounts, not active members.** Clarifying question resolved this as: server admins can ban an orphaned user (no org memberships) or block a known-bad email from ever being added to an org, not remove someone from an org they're actively in (that's what removing their org role already does). `User.is_banned`/`banned_at`/`banned_by` (self-referential FK) back new `ban_orphaned_user`/`unban_orphaned_user` endpoints (`routers/system.py`); `assign_org_role` now rejects a banned target outright, which is the actual enforcement point — a ban has no effect until someone tries to (re-)add that user to an org.

**Access Review renamed to "Users," with an organisation-visibility toggle.** Showing which organisations an orphaned/bannable user belongs to is genuinely useful triage context, but `SystemUserOut`'s own docstring documented org-name omission from this endpoint as a deliberate confidentiality boundary (I-M-05) — flagged explicitly rather than silently reversed. Clarifying question resolved it as a platform environment variable, defaulting to showing real names ("server admins can always look in the database anyway"): new `access_review_show_org_names` setting (`config.py`, default `True`) gates whether `SystemUserOut.organization_names` is populated or withheld, with the corresponding SOC 2 confidentiality-boundary note left in place rather than deleted, since the gap (a false setting still exposes `organization_count`) is now a documented, deliberate trade-off rather than an oversight.

**Component/category became a real two-level tree, not two independent flat lists.** Previously a requirement's category was chosen independently of its component (both project-wide siblings); the request's own example (core as the component, general as the category, from `docs/requirements.md`) made the intended shape unambiguous. `ProjectCategory.component_id` (new FK to `project_components.id`, `ondelete=CASCADE`) moves the uniqueness constraint from `(project_id, prefix)` to `(component_id, prefix)`. Migration policy — since existing multi-component categories are ambiguous about which component they "really" belonged to — was put to the user rather than guessed: full reorganisation, no backward-compatibility shim, confirmed against this project's own pre-release convention (`alembic/versions/0001_initial.py`'s docstring: one squashed baseline migration, no real deployments yet to preserve an incremental path for) that this only needed applying prospectively to `seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` (both reworked to build nested `dict[component_key][category_key]` lookups) plus a schema reset, not a generic historical backfill script.
