**Org-branded login's 2FA branch was a silent dead end**: `OrgLoginPage.tsx`'s own code comment claimed a 2FA challenge on an org-branded (`/login/:slug`) page "falls through to the plain /login flow rather than duplicating the code-entry form here" — but `handleSubmit` only ever branched on the non-2FA case; when `login()` returned `requires2fa: true`, nothing happened at all. No redirect, no error, no code-entry form — the submit button just stopped showing its loading state and the same email/password form sat there with zero indication anything needed to happen next. Fixed by actually implementing the documented fallback: `OrgLoginPage.tsx` now calls `navigate("/login", { state: { challengeToken: result.challengeToken } })` on the 2FA branch, and `LoginPage.tsx` reads an optional `challengeToken` out of router state to initialise its own `challengeToken` state, so it opens straight on the code-entry step instead of asking for email/password a second time (the already-issued challenge token is a plain string good for one `verify2fa` call regardless of which page holds it, so no backend change was needed).

**Verified**: `cd frontend && npx tsc -b` clean. New specs: `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/mobile-card-grid-overflow.spec.ts` (asserts `document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= clientWidth` at a 375×900 viewport across all four affected tile/card views — all 4 passed) and `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/org-login-2fa-handoff.spec.ts` (gives an org a slug via API, enrols the zero-org-membership "orphan" persona in real TOTP 2FA — same persona/pattern `two-factor-auth.spec.ts` already uses so no shared persona is left in a 2FA-enabled state for other specs — then drives a real browser through the org-branded page to confirm it lands on `/login`'s code-entry step rather than the pre-fix dead end; disables 2FA again at the end). Both run against a rebuilt/recreated `tests/container` frontend image. `golden-path.spec.ts` and `mobile-nav-rail.spec.ts` re-run clean, confirming no regression from either change. One unrelated hiccup during verification: the shared "orphan" persona was found already deactivated (`is_active: false`) from an earlier, unrelated test run against this long-lived dev stack — reactivated via `POST /api/v1/system/users/{id}/reactivate` before the new 2FA spec would run; not a defect in this change, just the same "continuously-mutating shared database" caveat `docs/e2e-workflows.md` already documents. Files changed: `frontend/src/pages/RequirementsPage.tsx`, `ChangeRequestsPage.tsx`, `ProjectListPage.tsx`, `FavouritesPage.tsx`, `OrgLoginPage.tsx`, `LoginPage.tsx`; new specs `mobile-card-grid-overflow.spec.ts`, `org-login-2fa-handoff.spec.ts`.

## UX audit and style guide: navigation, settings hierarchy, create flows, confirmation, terminology, and scale

The three bug fixes above were incidental discoveries from a broader, user-requested UX audit ("assume the role of a team manager for a team of UX designers"), run in four passes covering every page's navigation, tabs/accordions/modals, create-entity flows, destructive-action confirmation, platform-default overrides, multi-org navigation, auth screens, accessibility, copy/icon/badge consistency, terminology-override coverage, and list pagination against the app's own U-P-06 recommendation.

**New durable output — [docs/ux-style-guide.md](ux-style-guide.md)**: nine principles (settings-hierarchy depth model, override visibility, create-as-a-layer, one-component-per-pattern, one door for single/bulk create, two-tier confirmation, feedback-on-every-mutation, accessible-control-naming, wayfinding) plus five worked patterns (settings hierarchy, platform-default overrides, create panels/popovers/bulk, confirmation tiers + toast, an org-switcher wayfinding pattern modelled on Azure/Entra's tenant switcher) and a token reference documenting `frontend/src/styles/theme.css`'s existing colour/spacing system as the design system it already functions as, rather than proposing a new one. Normative for new/reworked frontend UI going forward.

**New point-in-time output — [docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md](ux-audit-2026-08.md)**: the findings that motivated each style-guide rule, plus an effort-sorted roadmap (24 items, S/M/L) tracked with a Status column. The two most severe findings, both surfaced only on the fourth pass: (1) the project-level terminology-override system (rename "requirement"/"change request"/"project"/"stage"/"component"/"category" per project) is consumed in only 5 call sites across 3 files — 4 of the 6 configurable terms have zero effect anywhere, and even the 2 partially-working ones leak the hardcoded English noun back in on the requirement detail page, comments, review-due lists, the CSV import wizard, and (most visibly) Project Admin's own custom-fields entity-kind dropdown, one tab over from the Terminology settings meant to rename those exact words; (2) two list views — a project's full change-history/audit timeline and the server-admin's cross-org user directory — have no `limit`/`offset` pagination on either the frontend request or the backend endpoint, in tension with `docs/requirements.md` U-P-06's (Recommended-priority) call for lazy-loading large data sets, while `ProjectListPage` already implements the same pattern correctly elsewhere in the app.

Neither finding was acted on in this pass — per the user's own explicit sequencing ("audit first, small fix now"), only the three small, unambiguous bugs above were fixed immediately; the structural findings (terminology coverage, list pagination, the org switcher, the Org Admin/Project Admin restructuring, and the new shared `Tabs`/`SidePanel`/`Popover`/`Modal`-confirm/`Toast` components) are scoped as roadmap items for a deliberate follow-up implementation pass, not silently deferred — see `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s Roadmap table for the full, current-as-of-this-entry list and status of each.

**Verified**: both new docs' Mermaid diagrams checked for balanced fences and valid node/edge syntax (reused, unmodified, from the diagrams already validated in the interactive review artifact this pass distilled from). `README.md` updated: the intro doc-links paragraph now points to both new files, and Known Limitations gets a one-sentence pointer to the audit's headline gaps (settings-hierarchy inconsistency, confirmation inconsistency, terminology coverage, unpaginated lists) rather than restating them. No application code changed in this entry — see the entry immediately above for the three code fixes this same audit pass produced.

## UX style guide: illustrated current-vs-proposed mockups, and phase 1 of the implementation roadmap

Two follow-ups to the UX audit/style guide entry above, same day.

**Illustrated current-vs-proposed mockups for `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s five patterns.** The user asked for real before/after images in the style guide itself, not just ASCII wireframes — matching the polished visual mockups already built for the interactive review artifact, but explicitly as illustrative mocks rather than literal app screenshots. Reused that artifact's exact mock-shot HTML/CSS markup (styled with the product's own light-theme token values) in a standalone local HTML file (nine `current`/`proposed` pairs: settings hierarchy, platform-default override, new-group popover, bulk-entry-point split button, change-request creation, two confirmation tiers, post-mutation toast feedback, and the org switcher), then rendered each pair to a PNG via a short headless-Chromium Playwright script (`chromium.launch()` + per-element `.screenshot()`, 2x device-scale-factor) using the `playwright` package already installed for `tests/playwright/`. Saved to `docs/figures/ux-style-guide/` (new directory, following the existing `docs/figures/architecture/` convention) and embedded in `docs/ux-style-guide.md` via standard Markdown image syntax, each with descriptive alt text, replacing the ASCII-art placeholders from the first pass. Two of the nine were initially clipped (the new-group popover's Cancel/Create row and the type-to-confirm dialog's buttons, both cut off by their mock container's `overflow: hidden` combined with an undersized `min-height` for absolutely-positioned content) — fixed by widening the containers and, for the popover, switching to `overflow: visible`, then re-rendered and visually re-verified. Total image payload ~1.0MB across 9 files, reasonable for a git-committed doc. No source `.html`/`.cjs` scratch files were committed — both lived in the session scratchpad and were discarded after use, matching how the interactive review artifact itself was never checked into the repo. Per the user's follow-up correction, the prose immediately surrounding each image was then trimmed to only what isn't already visible in the picture itself (implementation implications, disambiguating a hypothetical panel from a real one) — these mockups get the same "screenshots don't need a full explainer" treatment `CLAUDE.md` already grants README images, since the current/proposed labels and captions baked into each image already carry the reading key a diagram would otherwise need spelled out in surrounding text.

**Implementation, phase 1: the roadmap's S-effort quick wins.** Started work against `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s roadmap, beginning with the smallest, self-contained, no-design-decision items first (before the larger shared-component/backend/architectural items, which get scoped with the user before starting): `ReportsPage.tsx` gained the `Spinner` loading guard every other list-driven page already had (`!project || !reportConfig` — it was the one page of those audited that rendered empty dropdowns/rich-text fields until its `Promise.all` resolved); `RequirementDetailPage.tsx`'s archive button now calls `window.confirm(strings.requirements.archiveConfirm)` before archiving, matching `ActionDetailPage.tsx`'s existing behaviour for the structurally identical action (new `archiveConfirm` string added next to the existing `archive` button-label string in `strings.ts`, confirmed against `backend/app/routers/requirements.py::delete_requirement`'s own docstring — "Archives (soft-deletes)... preserving its history" — so the confirm copy accurately describes it as reversible-via-history rather than a true delete).

**Verified**: `cd frontend && npx tsc -b` clean. New spec `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts` — creates a throwaway requirement (never touches seeded data, since archiving isn't reversible from the UI), confirms cancelling the dialog leaves it in place, confirms accepting it archives and returns to the list, and asserts the dialog's exact message text. Initial version raced `page.waitForEvent("dialog")` against the triggering `.click()` inside one `Promise.all` and hung — native `confirm()` blocks the page's JS thread until resolved, so `.click()` never settled; fixed by switching to this repo's own established convention (`page.once("dialog", ...)` registered before the click, seen already in `personal-access-tokens.spec.ts`/`server-org-lifecycle.spec.ts`/`user-directory-and-bans.spec.ts`) rather than inventing a new pattern. Existing report-generation coverage (`report-image-attachment.spec.ts`) re-run clean against the `ReportsPage.tsx` change. `golden-path.spec.ts`, `mobile-nav-rail.spec.ts`, and `mobile-card-grid-overflow.spec.ts` re-run clean, confirming no regression. Both changes rebuilt/recreated in the `tests/container` frontend image before testing, per this repo's "containers don't bind-mount source" convention. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s roadmap table Status column updated to "Done" for both items. Files changed: `frontend/src/pages/ReportsPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/RequirementDetailPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; new spec `requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts`.

**Correction, same day: the "org switcher" finding and its Principle 9/wayfinding pattern were wrong, not just imprecise.** The user pushed back immediately on reading it: `ProjectListPage` and everything scoped under a project already deliberately pool across every org a user belongs to (an org column/filter is surfaced only when there's more than one org to disambiguate), so an Azure/Entra-style tenant switcher — which exists specifically to scope an *entire portal* to one tenant at a time — was the wrong model to reach for. It would have suggested exclusive org-scoping the app doesn't have and, per the roadmap item it produced, would have needed a new `CurrentOrgContext` threaded through the whole frontend to solve a problem that didn't actually exist. Re-examined and corrected rather than left in place: the real, much narrower gap is that `/orgs` (the only path to org *administration*, as opposed to content browsing) has no nav-rail entry point for anyone but a server admin — a one-link fix, no new state or component. Updated: `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s Principle 9 and its "Pattern: wayfinding" section (rewritten, no longer proposing a header switcher), `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s "at a glance" row (severity dropped High → Medium, description corrected) and its "Navigation & the orphan page" section (the incorrect framing left in place with an explicit note explaining what was wrong and why, rather than silently rewritten, so a reader following the document's own history can see the correction happened), and the roadmap row (L-effort "org switcher + CurrentOrgContext" replaced with S-effort "nav-rail entry point to `/orgs`"). The now-inaccurate `figures/ux-style-guide/wayfinding-org-switcher.png` mockup was deleted rather than left orphaned. No code was affected — the org-switcher item was never implemented, only documented, so this is a docs-only correction.

## Style guide binding on future work; implementation phase 1 completed (nav link, RichTextEditor Modal, aria-label sweep); three regressions found and fixed during full-suite verification

Two more pieces of the same day's work. First, per explicit user request, [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) gained a new "Frontend UX Style Guide Adherence" section (mirroring the existing SOC 2 policy section's binding language) making `docs/ux-style-guide.md` binding on new/reworked frontend UI for this and future agent sessions, not just descriptive — including a pointer to check the audit's roadmap before building a shared component from scratch, so the "newest feature already reproduces the pattern" problem the audit found doesn't keep recurring.

Second, the remaining phase-1 (S-effort) roadmap items were implemented: the corrected nav-rail link to `/orgs` (`Layout.tsx`, new `strings.nav.myOrganizations(orgPlural)` terminology-aware label, `Building2` icon reused from the existing server-admin equivalent — placed unconditionally in the Global section rather than gated on org-admin role, since determining that client-side would need new data-fetching the corrected finding explicitly argued against); `RichTextEditor.tsx`'s link-insert `window.prompt()` replaced with the shared `Modal` (a `linkModalOpen`/`linkUrl` state pair, a small form with a disabled-until-non-empty Insert button, `exec("createLink", ...)` unchanged — the file already had no `strings.ts` usage for its sibling image-picker panel, so plain literals were used here too rather than introducing a new convention for one component); and the aria-label sweep, delegated to a background agent scoped to exactly six files (`OrgAdminPage.tsx`, `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`, `RequirementDetailPage.tsx`, `CommentThread.tsx`, `ReportChapterListEditor.tsx`, `FileAttachmentList.tsx`) with the established `title`+matching-`aria-label` convention, sourcing text from `strings.ts` where a suitable key already existed and adding six new ones (`admin.removeMember`, `admin.removeNestedGroup`, `admin.deleteCustomField`, `orgAdmin.deleteReportTemplate`, `orgAdmin.deleteResource`, `orgAdmin.removeSsoMapping`) where none did — reviewed by reading its diffs directly rather than trusting its summary alone, per this session's own practice.

**Verification surfaced three real regressions**, distinct from the pre-existing "full-suite ordering collision" flakiness `docs/e2e-workflows.md` already documents (confirmed as that separate, expected category by rerunning each flaky spec alone against a fresh reseed, where all of them passed) — these three did not:
1. `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` clicked the requirement Archive button and asserted immediate navigation, with no dialog handler — broken by this same day's earlier archive-confirm addition. Fixed by adding `page.once("dialog", (dialog) => void dialog.accept())` before the click, matching the pattern `requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts` already established.
2. `org-label.spec.ts` asserted `getByRole("link", { name: "Tenants" })` (no `exact`) expecting one match; the new "My organisations" nav link, once the deployment's org label is overridden to "Tenants," renders as "My tenants" — a second element containing "Tenants" as a substring, so the non-exact locator now resolved to two elements. Fixed by adding `exact: true` to both affected assertions in that spec (a third, pre-existing assertion for the reverted-to-default case already had it, which is presumably why that one direction was never caught).
3. `mobile-card-grid-overflow.spec.ts`'s own two tile-view tests switched the shared `orgAdminAlphaBeta` persona's Requirements/Change-Requests view-mode preference to "tiles" — a server-persisted setting (`useViewMode`), not page-local state — and never switched it back, which later broke `project-history.spec.ts`'s shared `openRequirementByCode` helper (`.card, tr` combined selector, apparently not tile-view-safe) for any spec run afterward using that persona. Fixed by adding a "List view" click at the end of both tests, matching the restore-state convention this same file's own "Favourites card grid" test already used for its favourite toggle.

**Verified**: `cd frontend && npx tsc -b` clean throughout. Full Storybook suite (`npx vitest run --project=storybook`) — 47 files / 303 tests passed after merging the agent's changes with this session's own. Docker `tests/container` frontend image rebuilt/recreated. Per this repo's own documented methodology ("the only valid verification is a full clean-slate reseed followed by exactly one suite run"), the full Playwright suite was run three times against three separate `docker compose down -v && up --build -d` clean-slate resets: run 1 (63 specs, both `seed_e2e_dataset.py` and eventually `seed_demo_data.py`) surfaced the `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` regression plus 6 specs in the "ordering collision" category (one of which, `badge-filters.spec.ts`, turned out to be a self-inflicted gap in this run's own setup — it depends on `seed_demo_data.py`, which the first reset hadn't run, per `docs/e2e-workflows.md`'s own documented "how to run it" recipe); run 2 (both seed scripts run, regression #1 fixed) surfaced regressions #2 and #3; run 3, with all three regressions fixed and both seed scripts run: **62 passed, 1 failed** (`project-admin-templates-and-reports.spec.ts`, confirmed via an isolated rerun to pass cleanly alone — the same pre-existing ordering-collision category, not a fourth regression). `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s roadmap Status column updated to "Done" for the three items completed in this entry. Files changed: `frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx`, `RichTextEditor.tsx`, `RichTextEditor.stories.tsx`, `Layout.stories.tsx`, `i18n/strings.ts`, plus the six aria-label files listed above; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/org-admin-nav-link.spec.ts` (new), `richtext-insert-link-modal.spec.ts` (new), `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`, `org-label.spec.ts`, `mobile-card-grid-overflow.spec.ts`; `CLAUDE.md`.

## `OverridePill`: the platform-default/override pattern applied to the org branding form's text fields

Next roadmap item after the previous entry's three: "Reset affordance for text-based branding overrides" (header title, the three email-footer fields — accent colour already had a working revert checkbox per the audit, and logo/login-background need a new backend `DELETE` endpoint, so both stayed out of this frontend-only pass).

**New shared `frontend/src/components/OverridePill.tsx`**, per the style guide's "Pattern: platform default vs. override" — one component, not four ad hoc implementations, since this exact field shape (header title, footer company/website/address) repeats four times on one form. Renders a `Platform default` badge, or a `Custom` badge plus an optional `onReset` button; `custom` is driven by the *saved* org value (`org.header_title != null`, etc.), not the in-progress input, so it can't flicker mid-edit or claim a state that isn't actually persisted yet — clicking Reset only clears the field locally, the existing shared "Save branding" button is still what persists it, matching how every other field on this form already batches edits rather than saving per-field.

**Restructured the four affected fields from an implicit `<label>` wrapping input+hint to an explicit `htmlFor`/`id` pair**, label and `OverridePill` as row siblings above the input. The implicit-wrap shape (still used elsewhere on this same form, e.g. the accent-colour checkbox) would have nested `OverridePill`'s own `<button>` inside the `<label>` associated with the *input*, which is invalid: a label's click-forwarding targets its associated control, and stacking an unrelated interactive `<button>` inside that same label is exactly the kind of nested-interactive-element structure accessibility tooling flags. Confirmed the restructure doesn't change accessible-name computation `getByLabelText` depends on (`explicit `htmlFor`/`id` computes the same way implicit wrapping does) by keeping the existing `BrandingSectionSave`/`BrandingSectionEmailFooterSave` stories' assertions (`getByLabelText(/^Header title/)`, `getByLabelText("Company name")`, `getByLabelText("Website")`) unchanged and green.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` clean. New `OverridePill.stories.tsx` (5 stories: platform-default state, custom-with-reset, custom-without-reset, both themes) plus a new `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx` story (`BrandingSectionOverridePillAndReset`, using a new `org` override option added to the existing `mockOrgAdminApis` test helper) exercising both pill states and the reset-then-clear flow against a header-title-and-company-name-customised fixture — full Storybook suite 48 files / 309 tests, all green. New Playwright spec `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`, deliberately written against the seeded Alpha org rather than fixture data, since `seed_e2e_dataset.py` already customises Alpha's email-footer fields while leaving its header title on the platform default — exercises both pill states with zero extra setup, resets the company name field through the real UI, confirms the pill flips to Platform default after Save+reload, and restores the org's original seeded branding in `afterEach` (runs even on failure) so this shared org's data isn't left mutated for later spec runs — confirmed by re-running the same spec back-to-back, which only passes a second time if the first run's cleanup actually worked. One bug in the first draft of that spec: a second `.click()` on the "Branding section" toggle after `page.reload()` *collapsed* it instead of re-expanding it, since `CollapsibleSection`'s expand/collapse state is itself server-persisted per user (`useUiPreference`) and had stayed expanded across the reload — fixed by switching to this suite's own existing `ensureExpanded()` helper (checks `aria-expanded` before deciding whether to click) instead of an unconditional click, the same fix category `mobile-card-grid-overflow.spec.ts` needed for a different persisted-preference collision in the previous entry. Regression sweep (`golden-path`, `org-label`, `org-rename-and-test-email`, `org-security-controls`) re-run clean. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done". Files changed: `frontend/src/components/OverridePill.tsx` (new), `OverridePill.stories.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx`, `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`, `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; new spec `org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`.

## `Modal`: focus trap and focus restoration

Next S-effort roadmap item: `Modal`'s ARIA roles were already correct (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, `aria-label`, Escape-to-close), but it had neither of the two behavioural gaps a real focus trap needs — Tab could walk a keyboard user out of the dialog into the page behind it, and closing never returned focus to whatever had triggered it.

**Both fixed in `Modal.tsx` alone** (used everywhere `Modal` is used already, no call-site changes needed): a `dialogRef` captures the dialog element; on mount, `document.activeElement` is saved for restoration on unmount, and initial focus moves to the first focusable descendant *only if nothing inside already has focus* — the qualifier matters because `RichTextEditor`'s link-insert form (added earlier this same day) already sets its own `autoFocus` on the URL field, and a naive "always focus the first element" would have stolen focus back to the dialog's own Close button out from under it, since passive `useEffect`s run after React's native `autoFocus` handling during commit. The existing Escape-key listener gained Tab-trap logic in the same handler: on `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` at either end of the dialog's own focusable-element list, `preventDefault` and wrap to the other end, rather than letting focus leave the dialog.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and `npx eslint` both clean. Four new `Modal.stories.tsx` stories: `FocusMovesIntoDialogOnOpen` (default children, no focusable content of their own → the Close button gets focus), `AutoFocusChildWins` (a child `<input autoFocus>` keeps focus, confirming the fallback correctly defers), `TabTrapCycles` (two extra buttons in children; Tab from the last wraps to Close, Shift+Tab from Close wraps to the last), `RestoresFocusOnClose` (a small stateful harness component that opens/closes the real `Modal`, confirming focus returns to the triggering button after Escape). Full Storybook suite: 48 files / 313 tests, all green. `tests/container` frontend image rebuilt/recreated; Playwright regression sweep (`richtext-insert-link-modal.spec.ts`, `change-request-tasks-and-voting.spec.ts`, `change-request-approval-separation.spec.ts`, `golden-path.spec.ts` — covering every real page that renders a `Modal` today) re-run clean, confirming the `autoFocus`-wins fallback actually holds in the live app, not just in an isolated story. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done". Files changed: `frontend/src/components/Modal.tsx`, `Modal.stories.tsx`.

## Shared `Tabs` component (migrated all three hand-rolled tab bars) and shared `Toast` component (piloted on three real mutations)

Two more M-effort roadmap items, worked in parallel — `Tabs` migration delegated to a background agent once the component itself and its tests were done, while `Toast` was built and piloted directly, since wiring it into real pages needed judgment calls (which mutations, what copy) a scripted sweep shouldn't make unsupervised.

**`Tabs`** (`frontend/src/components/Tabs.tsx`, new): WAI-ARIA APG "Tabs, automatic activation" — arrow keys both move focus and switch the active tab. Replaces `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`/`ServerManagementPage.tsx`/`PreferencesPage.tsx`'s three byte-for-byte-identical hand-rolled tab bars (confirmed identical by direct comparison before building anything — the audit's finding was exact, not approximate), none of which had `role="tablist"`/`"tab"` or keyboard nav beyond plain Tab-through. Renders the same markup/CSS as the hand-rolled version (a visual no-op), plus a `tabPanelProps(idPrefix, key)` helper callers spread onto each panel so `aria-controls`/`aria-labelledby` are real, not just the tablist half of the pattern. `idPrefix` is caller-supplied (not internally generated) specifically so the same string can be shared between the `Tabs` call and every `tabPanelProps` call without extra plumbing.

Initial implementation moved focus on arrow-key nav via `requestAnimationFrame` + `document.getElementById` after the state update — flaky in its own Storybook tests (a hardcoded `idPrefix="demo"` let one story's stale DOM answer another story's `getElementById` lookup) and, even after making `idPrefix` unique via `useId()`, still occasionally failed on the wrap-around case. Root-caused and replaced: every tab button is *always* rendered (only its panel is conditional), so the arrow-key target already exists in the DOM at keydown time — a `buttonRefs` ref array + a synchronous `.focus()` call inside the same event handler removed the whole class of timing bugs, and is also just more idiomatic React for this than an id-string round-trip.

`react-refresh/only-export-components` flagged `tabPanelProps` (a non-component export colocated with the `Tabs` component) — added to `eslint.config.js`'s `allowExportNames`, the project's own documented escape hatch for exactly this shape (already used for `resolveLandingPath`/`useViewMode`/etc.), not a new exception.

**Migration** (delegated to a background agent, reviewed after): `idPrefix`s `"project-admin-tabs"`/`"server-management-tabs"`/`"preferences-tabs"`. Also fixed, while touching the one file that had it: `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`'s `"reportSetup"` tab was the one label in the whole file hardcoded as a literal string rather than sourced from `strings.ts` (a separately-noted audit finding) — added `strings.admin.reportSetup` and switched the tabs array to use it. `Tabs` buttons now carry `role="tab"` (overriding the implicit `role="button"` every hand-rolled version had), which broke every Storybook/Playwright locator written as `getByRole("button", { name: "<tab label>" })` against one of these three pages — the agent fixed all affected Storybook selectors as part of its own verification; **the same class of breakage existed in 20 Playwright spec files** (not the agent's scope, found and fixed directly afterward): `getByRole("button", { name: "<label>" })` → `getByRole("tab", { name: "<label>" })` for the exact, exhaustively-enumerated set of 13 tab-label strings across ProjectAdminPage/ServerManagementPage/PreferencesPage, applied via a scoped `sed` sweep across the 20 confirmed files (every hit individually confirmed via `grep` first, not a blind regex) rather than by hand one at a time.

**`Toast`** (`frontend/src/context/ToastContext.tsx` + `.css` additions, new): a `ToastProvider`/`useToast()` pair, matching this codebase's existing context-module shape (`ThemeContext`, `AuthContext`) rather than an ad hoc singleton — mounted once in `main.tsx`, above `AuthProvider`. Success toasts reuse a solid dark chip (good contrast in both themes); error toasts follow `.btn-danger`'s own established convention of using the danger colour as an *accent*, never a solid fill (`border-left` stripe on a neutral surface) — matched to what's actually in this app already rather than the red-fill choice the review artifact's own illustrative mockups took a design liberty with. Auto-dismisses after 4s or on manual close; stacks multiple toasts; portalled to `document.body` like `Modal`/`Tooltip`. A `withToast()` Storybook decorator was added to `testing/storybook-helpers.tsx` alongside the existing `withAuth`/`withRouter` ones for any page whose stories need to assert a toast fired.

**Piloted on three real mutations**, not "every mutation" — the roadmap item's literal scope (dozens of call sites across ~20 files) is too large to do safely and reviewably in one pass; shipping the infrastructure plus a proven, tested pilot is the honest unit of work here, with the rest tracked as a follow-up rather than claimed done. Chosen pilots: `RequirementDetailPage.tsx`'s `archive()` (also gained a `try`/`catch` it never had before — one more of the audit's "no error handling at all" findings, fixed while already in this function), `ActionDetailPage.tsx`'s `archive()` (same treatment, for symmetry with its sibling), and `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s `saveBranding()` (kept its existing inline `brandingError` display for field-level context *and* added a toast, rather than one replacing the other — the style guide's Principle 7 asks for both a shared toast and a shared inline error state, not one instead of the other).

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint src/` both clean (the only warnings are the same 5-item pre-existing `BrandingContext.tsx`/`ThemeContext.tsx` baseline this repo already tracks — no new ones). Full Storybook suite: 50 files / 326 tests, including new stories for `Tabs` (click-switches-tab, tablist/tabpanel `aria-labelledby`/`aria-controls` cross-check, arrow-key nav with wraparound) and `Toast` (success, error, stacking, manual dismiss), plus toast assertions added to `RequirementDetailPage.stories.tsx`/`ActionDetailPage.stories.tsx`/`OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s existing archive/save stories.

**Also found and fixed, while verifying against the real app: a genuine pre-existing bug in the shared Playwright helper `openRequirementByCode`** (`tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/helpers.ts`), unrelated to anything built in this entry but surfaced by it — `project-history.spec.ts` failed in a full clean-slate suite run with a strict-mode violation resolving 9 links instead of 1. Root cause: the helper's `page.locator(".card, tr", { hasText: code }).first()` shape is broken whenever a project has enough requirements — `RequirementsPage.tsx`'s list view wraps its entire `<table>` in one outer `<div class="card">`, which also "has text" matching any code found in *any* row and sits before every `<tr>` in document order, so `.first()` silently resolved to that outer wrapper rather than the specific row; invisible with few requirements (the wrapper's one findable link and the correct row's link were the same element), a real failure once a full suite run's accumulated throwaway-requirement creation grew a project past that threshold. Fixed by locating the code's own exact text first, then walking up via XPath to its *nearest* `tr`/`.card` ancestor, rather than a flat multi-selector `hasText` match — eliminates the ambiguity structurally instead of coincidentally avoiding it. Re-verified: three full clean-slate-reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both `seed_e2e_dataset.py` and `seed_demo_data.py`) suite runs while chasing this and the two locator-migration sweeps above — first showed the `openRequirementByCode` failure alone (unrelated to Tabs/Toast), second (after the helper fix) showed it resolved with one unrelated later assertion failing from same-session data accumulation (confirmed by an isolated fresh-reseed rerun passing clean), **third and final: 64/64 passed.**

Files changed: `frontend/src/components/Tabs.tsx` (new), `Tabs.stories.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/context/ToastContext.tsx` (new), `ToastContext.stories.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/styles/theme.css`, `frontend/src/main.tsx`, `frontend/src/testing/storybook-helpers.tsx`, `frontend/eslint.config.js`, `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/ProjectAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `ServerManagementPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `PreferencesPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `RequirementDetailPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `ActionDetailPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `OrgAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`); `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/helpers.ts` and 20 spec files' tab-button locators. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated: `Tabs` → Done; `Toast` → partial, noted as "component built, piloted on 3 mutations, broader sweep is follow-up work" rather than marked fully Done.

## Shared `SidePanel` and `Popover` components, and the first real popover usage (Org Admin's "New group")

Next M-effort roadmap item, explicitly called out as a prerequisite for several others still queued (the single/bulk create entry points, "New X" copy unification) — style guide "Pattern: create panels, popovers, and one door for bulk": a full-height right-anchored `SidePanel` for multi-field create/edit flows too heavy for a popover, and a small anchored `Popover` for one- or two-field flows, replacing the "always-visible inline form pinned below the list" pattern the audit found on every one of the app's 14+ "new X" flows.

**Extracted `dialogA11y.ts`** (`useDialogA11y`, a plain `.ts` hook, not a component) out of `Modal.tsx` before writing either new component: `SidePanel` needs the exact same focus-trap/focus-restoration/Escape-to-close behaviour `Modal` already had, and `Popover` needs the trap/restore half of it too (it has no backdrop or Escape-only affordance of its own). Rather than copy the ~50 lines a second time, `Modal.tsx` was refactored to call the shared hook, with no behavioural change (its own stories — `FocusMovesIntoDialogOnOpen`, `AutoFocusChildWins`, `TabTrapCycles`, `RestoresFocusOnClose` — all still pass unmodified, confirming the extraction was a pure refactor).

**`SidePanel.tsx`** (new): structurally `Modal` with a different backdrop layout — `justifyContent: "flex-end"` instead of centred, full-height, `borderRadius: 0` — same header+title+Close-button chrome, same backdrop-click-to-close. No new a11y surface beyond what `useDialogA11y` already provides.

**`Popover.tsx`** (new): positioned relative to a caller-supplied `anchorRef` on the same principle as `Tooltip` (portalled to `document.body`, measured via `getBoundingClientRect` so a scrollable ancestor like the nav rail can't clip it, flips above the anchor when there's no room below) but click-triggered rather than hover-triggered, interactive, and — unlike `Tooltip` — closes on outside click as well as Escape (a `mousedown` listener that no-ops when the target is inside the bubble or the anchor itself, so the trigger button's own toggle click isn't double-handled). Has no header chrome of its own; the caller supplies the whole body, typically ending in its own Cancel/submit row that calls `onClose`. First implementation positioned via React state (`setPosition` in a `useLayoutEffect`, same shape as `Tooltip`) and failed two of its own Storybook a11y stories (`FocusMovesIntoPopoverOnOpen`, `RestoresFocusOnClose`) — focus landed on `<body>` instead of the popover's first field. Root cause: the state update added an extra render pass between mount and `useDialogA11y`'s passive focus effect, which raced it. Fixed by applying the measured position directly to the DOM node inside the same `useLayoutEffect` (`bubble.style.top/left/visibility`) instead of through React state — single render, no race, and arguably more correct for a value that's read once per open rather than needing to trigger further re-renders.

**First real usage: Org Admin's "New group."** Exactly the flow the style guide's own mockup (`figures/ux-style-guide/create-group-popover.png`) illustrates. `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s Organisation groups section previously had a permanently-visible `<input placeholder="Name">` + "New group" button pinned below the group list at all times; replaced with a "New group" button at the top of the section that opens a `Popover` with just the name field and a Cancel/Create row. `createGroup()` gained a `try`/`catch` it didn't have before, plus `showToast` on both success (`orgAdmin.groupCreated`, new string) and failure — Principle 7, applied here since the flow was already being touched, not a mechanical sweep of the rest of the app's create flows (`Toast`'s broader rollout is still tracked separately as partial).

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. Full Storybook suite: 52 files / 343 tests, including new `SidePanel.stories.tsx` (mirrors `Modal.stories.tsx`'s open/close/focus-trap/restore coverage) and `Popover.stories.tsx` (a stateful harness with a real trigger button and anchor, since there's no meaningful way to render `Popover` without one; covers open, Escape-close, outside-click-close, click-inside-does-not-close, focus-on-open, tab-trap, and restore-on-close — the last one deliberately starts the harness closed and opens via a real click, since restoration is only a meaningful assertion when the "previously focused element" `useDialogA11y` captures on mount is genuinely the trigger, not `<body>`), plus a new `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx` story (`GroupsSectionCreateGroupViaPopover`) exercising the full popover create flow including the toast. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image. Updated the one existing Playwright spec that drove the old inline form directly (`org-group-nesting.spec.ts`, which creates two throwaway groups as setup for its actual nesting assertions) to open the popover and submit through it instead — re-run clean, along with `org-label.spec.ts`, `org-security-controls.spec.ts`, `org-rename-and-test-email.spec.ts`, `org-admin-project-statuses-and-link-types.spec.ts`, `org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`, `org-admin-nav-link.spec.ts`. One unrelated failure surfaced first, on the (non-clean-slate) targeted rerun that included `org-admin-project-statuses-and-link-types.spec.ts`: the seeded Beta org had only 1 of its expected 4 project statuses, from state a prior spec run in this same long-lived dev stack had left behind — not caused by this change (confirmed by inspecting the failure's own accessibility snapshot, which showed the unrelated statuses missing, not a groups-section symptom). Per this repo's own documented methodology, ruled it out with a full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and exactly one full suite run rather than chasing it further: **64/64 passed.** `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done" for the `SidePanel`/`Popover` item. Files changed: `frontend/src/components/dialogA11y.ts` (new), `SidePanel.tsx` (new), `SidePanel.stories.tsx` (new), `Popover.tsx` (new), `Popover.stories.tsx` (new), `Modal.tsx`, `frontend/src/styles/theme.css`, `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/org-group-nesting.spec.ts`.

## Shared `ConfirmDialog` component (both confirmation tiers), piloted on three real call sites

Next M-effort roadmap item, and per the audit's own note the highest-severity one relative to its footprint: no shared confirmation component existed at all, so the app had accumulated four different patterns — nothing (most delete/archive buttons), `window.confirm` (~9 call sites, e.g. requirement/action archive, org disable, PAT revocation), and one ad hoc inline card with typed-name confirmation (organisation delete, the app's one existing irreversible/wide-blast-radius action) — instead of the style guide's two deliberate tiers.

**`ConfirmDialog.tsx`** (new): a single component for both tiers, not two — which tier renders is driven entirely by whether the caller passes `requireTypedText`, not a `variant` prop callers have to choose correctly. Wraps `Modal` (so it inherits its focus-trap/restore/Escape-to-close behaviour via `dialogA11y.ts` for free); Tier 1 renders a plain Cancel/confirm row, Tier 2 additionally renders a text input whose value must exactly match `requireTypedText` before the confirm button enables. `confirmLabel` is caller-supplied (e.g. "Archive", "Permanently delete") rather than a fixed "Confirm", matching how every other shared component in this codebase (`Modal`, `Popover`) takes its copy from the caller rather than baking in generic text.

**Piloted on three real call sites**, not a mechanical sweep of the ~12 remaining ones — same reasoning as `Toast`'s pilot (which mutation gets which exact copy, and which existing pattern it's replacing, is a judgement call per site):
- **Tier 1** — `RequirementDetailPage.tsx` and `ActionDetailPage.tsx`'s `archive()` actions, both previously `window.confirm`. `strings.requirements.archiveConfirm`/`strings.actions.archiveConfirm` were each split into a new short `archiveTitle` (the dialog's title/aria-label) and a trimmed `archiveConfirm` (now message-body text only, no longer prefixed with the question the title now carries).
- **Tier 2** — `ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx`'s organisation delete, previously a bespoke `.card` rendered inline below the table (its own `deleteConfirmText` state, a plain unlabelled `<input>`, manual `disabled={deleteConfirmText !== org.name}` wiring). Replaced with `<ConfirmDialog requireTypedText={org.name} .../>`; the page no longer needs its own `deleteConfirmText` state at all, since `ConfirmDialog` owns the typed value internally and only calls `onConfirm` once it already matches — the confirm handler just reads the already-known `org.name` rather than threading the typed string back out. The input also gained a real accessible name it never had before (`aria-label` from a new `strings.common.typeToConfirmLabel(name)` helper — `Type "Acme Corp" to confirm` — Principle 8), on top of the existing `placeholder`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. Full Storybook suite: 53 files / 350 tests — new `ConfirmDialog.stories.tsx` (Tier 1 confirm/cancel, Tier 2 disabled-until-exact-match), plus `RequirementDetailPage.stories.tsx`/`ActionDetailPage.stories.tsx`'s existing archive-toast stories rewritten to drive the in-app dialog instead of `spyOn(window, "confirm")` (each gained a paired cancel-leaves-it-untouched story), and `ServerOrganisationsPage.stories.tsx`'s `DeleteRequiresTypedConfirmation` rewritten to query `within(document.body)` for the dialog rather than `within(canvasElement)`, since `Modal` (and therefore `ConfirmDialog`) portals out of the page root. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image. Updated the two Playwright specs that drove the old native/inline patterns directly: `requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts` (rewritten from `page.once("dialog", ...)` native-dialog handling to `page.getByRole("dialog")` against the in-app one) and `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` (same swap, at the one other spot in the suite that archived a requirement past its confirmation step). `server-org-lifecycle.spec.ts`'s existing org-delete step needed no changes — it already located the confirm input by `getByPlaceholder(orgName)`, which still resolves correctly since `Modal`'s portal target is `document.body` and Playwright's `page.` locators aren't scoped to a container the way Storybook's `within(canvasElement)` is. One targeted (non-clean-slate) rerun surfaced `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` failing on an *earlier*, unrelated step than the one this change touches (its own seeded/renamed locked requirement no longer existed under its original name, from that same spec already having run — and permanently mutated it — earlier in the session); ruled out as pre-existing non-idempotency rather than a regression by re-running the full suite once against a fresh clean-slate reseed per this repo's own documented methodology: **64/64 passed.** `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Partial" for this item (component built and proven on both tiers in production; broader rollout across the remaining ~12 call sites is follow-up work, tracked the same honest way `Toast` is). Files changed: `frontend/src/components/ConfirmDialog.tsx` (new), `ConfirmDialog.stories.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/pages/RequirementDetailPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `ActionDetailPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts`, `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`.

## One entry point for single vs. bulk requirement creation

Next M-effort roadmap item — style guide "Pattern: create panels, popovers, and one door for bulk": `RequirementsPage.tsx` had the inline "New requirement" form (toggle-to-reveal) and `CsvImportWizard`'s own "Import CSV"/"Export CSV"/"Download template" row rendered **unconditionally**, competing as two separate, permanently-visible blocks above the list — the audit's own example of "zero of the fourteen create-entity flows use a modal, drawer, or popover."

**Retitled from the roadmap's original "...requirement/change-request creation."** Change Requests has no bulk/CSV creation path at all (confirmed against the audit's own "How things get created" table) — there is nothing to consolidate there under this item. Its inline form staying inline (not becoming a `SidePanel`) is an explicit scoping decision, not an oversight; converting it is a separate, not-yet-ticketed future step if the team wants it.

**`CsvImportWizard.tsx`**: converted to `forwardRef` exposing a new `CsvImportWizardHandle` (`{ openFilePicker: () => void }`, via `useImperativeHandle`) and a new `showImportTrigger?: boolean` prop (default `true`, so any future caller gets today's self-contained behaviour without opting in). When `false`, its own "Import CSV" label+button don't render, but the underlying `<input type="file">` still does (now always rendered once, referenced by `fileInputRef`, and conditionally wrapped in the visible label or left bare) — a caller can trigger the OS file picker via the ref without either component needing to reach into the other's state. Export/template stay visible unconditionally either way, in both callers — they're downloads, not part of the create flow this pattern is about, so folding them into the same "Add" menu as "Add one"/"Import from CSV" would have grouped semantically unrelated actions together.

**`RequirementsPage.tsx`**: the old unconditional `<CsvImportWizard/>` render now passes `showImportTrigger={false}` and a `ref`. The header's "New {requirement}" button now opens a `Popover` with two items — "Add one" (unchanged: sets `showNewForm(true)`, same inline form as before) and "Import from CSV" (`csvWizardRef.current?.openFilePicker()`). The deep-link path from Project Overview's own "New requirement" button (`?new=1` query param) is untouched — it still calls `setShowNewForm(true)` directly on mount, bypassing the popover entirely, since a user who already clicked "New requirement" on a different page shouldn't have to choose "Add one" again on arrival.

**Mid-task addition to `CLAUDE.md`, per explicit user request**: a new bullet in "Frontend UX Style Guide Adherence" generalising the principle this exact piece of work is an instance of — before building new frontend UI, check whether an equivalent already exists elsewhere in the codebase; if it does but isn't a shared component yet, extract it into one *and update the existing call site(s)*, not just the new one. Landing a new page on a shared component while leaving the original bespoke copy in place adds a second inconsistent implementation instead of removing the first.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. Full Storybook suite: 53 files / 352 tests — new `CsvImportWizard.stories.tsx` story (`HiddenImportTriggerOpensViaRef`, a small ref-holding harness proving the file picker opens via the imperative handle with no visible "Import CSV" trigger, and that the rest of the import flow — upload, mapping, confirm — is otherwise identical) and new `RequirementsPage.stories.tsx` story (`NewRequirementMenuOffersOneOrBulk`); three existing `RequirementsPage.stories.tsx` stories that clicked "New" expecting the inline form to appear immediately updated to click "Add one" in the resulting popover first. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image. Updated seven Playwright specs whose "New requirement"/"New Requirement" click previously opened the inline form directly: `golden-path.spec.ts`, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, `requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts`, `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`, `review-scheduling.spec.ts`, `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` — each gained one `page.getByRole("button", { name: "Add one" }).click()` immediately after the existing trigger click; `csv-import-wizard.spec.ts` needed no changes, since its `page.locator('input[type="file"]...').setInputFiles(...)` targets the file input directly regardless of visibility, and `page.` locators aren't scoped to a rendering container the way Storybook's `within(canvasElement)` is. Two targeted (non-clean-slate) reruns surfaced failures entirely unrelated to this change and upstream of anything it touches — `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` blocked on a disabled "New field" button before ever reaching its Requirements-page step (stale custom-field-name collision from that same non-idempotent spec's own earlier run), and `csv-import-wizard.spec.ts`'s second test failed creating a requirement via direct API call (`createResp.ok()` false, same non-idempotency category) — both consistent with this repo's documented "state-mutating specs need a fresh reseed to rerun cleanly" limitation, not a regression. Ruled out with a full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and one full suite run: **63/64 passed**, the one failure (`project-admin-templates-and-reports.spec.ts`, a Gamma-org report-template flow with zero relation to Requirements/CSV/Popover) confirmed via an isolated rerun to pass cleanly alone — the same pre-existing full-suite ordering-collision category `docs/e2e-workflows.md` already documents, and the exact same spec that landed in that category two entries above in this log. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done", retitled to drop the change-request half per the scoping note above. Files changed: `frontend/src/components/CsvImportWizard.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/RequirementsPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`; `tests/playwright/tests/golden-path.spec.ts`, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/review-scheduling.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`.

## Consolidate filter UI onto `FilterPanel`: Project List, Project Reviews Due, Server Organisations, Notifications

Next M-effort roadmap item — the audit's own "Pattern inconsistency" finding: `RequirementsPage.tsx`/`ChangeRequestsPage.tsx` already established a `.side-grid` shell (list on the left, a right-hand `FilterPanel` sidebar of `FilterField`/`FilterCheckbox` controls) but four other list pages kept their filters as an inline row of buttons/selects above the list instead, a second, inconsistent pattern for the same job.

**User was explicitly asked first whether the separately-flagged, much larger "terminology overrides" item (the audit's other High-severity finding, needing `strings.ts` itself to become terminology-aware per the audit's own suggested end-state) should be tackled now or deferred — user said to do it last, as a full architectural fix, after every other roadmap item.** `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s terminology-overrides row updated to record that explicit ordering decision before moving on to this item.

**Convention split, matched exactly to what `RequirementsPage.tsx` already does**: search inputs, page-level actions ("New project", "Mark all read"), and the `ViewToggle` stay in the main content area's own header row — only controls that *narrow* the visible set move into `FilterPanel`. This is a judgment call made once against the reference pages rather than re-derived per page:
- **`ProjectListPage.tsx`**: the Active/Archived toggle (previously two buttons) became a `FilterField` select (`Status`: Active/Archived); organisation, role, and stage-status filters moved from their own inline `<select>`s into `FilterField`s unchanged otherwise. Search and `ViewToggle` stayed in the main row.
- **`ProjectReviewsDuePage.tsx`**: component and reviewer selects moved into two `FilterField`s. No search/actions existed on this page to begin with.
- **`ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx`**: the three-way Active/Disabled/All button group became a single `FilterField` select. Search, "New {org}", and the create-org form/import-warnings/action-error blocks stayed in the main content column, above the table.
- **`NotificationsPage.tsx`**: the "Unread only" checkbox moved from the inline row into `FilterPanel` via `FilterCheckbox`, unchanged otherwise. Search and "Mark all read" stayed in the main row.

All four pages now wrap their content in the same `.side-grid` CSS class `RequirementsPage.tsx` uses (defined once in `theme.css`: `1fr 240px` two-column grid, collapsing to one column under the existing narrow-viewport breakpoint) — no new CSS, reusing the existing shell exactly.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. Storybook: all four pages' existing stories (23 combined) passed unmodified except one, confirming the refactor is behaviour-preserving — `ServerOrganisationsPage.stories.tsx`'s `ShowAllIncludesDisabled` clicked a `getByRole("button", { name: "All" })` that no longer exists now that status is a select; updated to `userEvent.selectOptions(canvas.getByLabelText("Status"), "All")`. `FilterPanel`/`FilterField`/`FilterCheckbox` already had their own dedicated component-level Storybook coverage from when `RequirementsPage.tsx` first introduced them, so no new stories were needed for the shared component itself — only the one page-level story broken by the button-to-select change. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image. Grepped every Playwright spec for interactions with the four pages' filters before running anything: `server-org-lifecycle.spec.ts` had the same "All" button click as the broken story (fixed to `page.getByLabel("Status").selectOption("all")`); `notifications-and-favourites.spec.ts`'s `page.getByLabel("Unread only")` and `project-list-org-scoping.spec.ts`'s CSS locator against the organisation `<select>`'s own `<option>` text both needed no changes, since neither depends on where in the DOM the control sits. Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and one full Playwright suite run: 63/64 passed, the one failure (`report-image-attachment.spec.ts`, a report intro image-upload/PDF-generation spec with zero relation to any of the four pages touched) confirmed via an isolated rerun to pass cleanly alone — a pre-existing timing flake, not a regression. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done." Files changed: `frontend/src/pages/ProjectListPage.tsx`, `ProjectReviewsDuePage.tsx`, `ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `NotificationsPage.tsx`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/server-org-lifecycle.spec.ts`.

## A visible "Invite" entry point + keyboard navigation for `UserAutocomplete`

Next M-effort roadmap item — the audit's "fifteenth create flow with no visible trigger at all" finding: inviting an external, non-org-member user to a project is real and working (Project Admin's Groups tab), but there's no "Invite" button anywhere and no visual cue the field does anything beyond adding an existing member — the only entry point is typing an email and noticing what the dropdown does. The component itself also had zero keyboard support (no arrow keys, no Enter-to-select), the style guide's own Principle 4 example of "every interactive control has a name" / arrow-key navigation.

**Scope check first**: `UserAutocomplete` has 3 call sites (`OrgAdminPage.tsx` ×2, `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` ×1), but only the `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` one passes `organizationId`+`onSelectExternal` — the other two only add existing org members to a group, with no invite capability at all. So the "visible entry point" half of this fix is scoped to that one call site; the keyboard-navigation half applies to the component as a whole (used everywhere) — including making the invite-only affordances (the hint text, the invite option in the dropdown) opt-in based on whether the caller wired up `organizationId`/`onSelectExternal`, so the two non-invite-capable call sites are unaffected.

**`UserAutocomplete.tsx`**: rebuilt around the WAI-ARIA "Combobox with list autocomplete" pattern. The input gets `role="combobox"`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, `aria-autocomplete="list"`, `aria-activedescendant`; the dropdown is `role="listbox"`; each match (including the invite/add-existing result) is `role="option"` with a matching `id`/`aria-selected`, plus a highlighted-row background so the same state is visible, not just announced. Arrow Up/Down move a `highlightedIndex` through the combined `matches` + external-match option list (wrapping at the ends, opening the dropdown on the first Down if closed); Enter selects whatever's highlighted; Escape closes without selecting. A new `inviteCapable` flag (`organizationId && onSelectExternal` both set) drives a persistent hint paragraph — "Not a member yet? Type their email to invite them." — shown before any query is typed, so the invite path is visible without already knowing the trick; it steps aside once typing starts, replaced by the dropdown's own "Invite {email}"/"Add {email}" result once a query resolves one. Deliberately kept separate from `canInvite` (whether *this specific query* actually resolved an external match) — the hint's job is discoverability up front, not confirming a match exists.

**`ProjectAdminPage.tsx`**: the one invite-capable call site's placeholder changed from `strings.admin.addMemberPlaceholder` ("Type a name or email to add…") to a new `strings.admin.addOrInviteMemberPlaceholder` ("Type a name to add, or an email to invite…") — the other two (non-invite-capable) call sites keep the original placeholder, since "or an email to invite" would be actively misleading there.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. `UserAutocomplete.stories.tsx`: 3 existing stories pass unmodified (behaviour-preserving), plus 3 new ones — `KeyboardNavigationSelectsHighlightedOption` (Arrow-Arrow-Enter selects the second match, no mouse), `EscapeClosesTheDropdown`, `InviteHintVisibleBeforeTyping` (hint shown before typing, gone once a query is entered). Full Storybook suite: 54 files / 365 tests. Two page-level stories needed fixes for a real, expected side effect of making the input itself `role="combobox"`: `ProjectAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s `GroupsTabAddOrgGroup` had a bare `getByRole("combobox")` that was unambiguous before (only the org-group-nesting `<select>` had that role) and is now ambiguous (`UserAutocomplete`'s own input on the same tab matches too) — scoped to `.find((el) => el.tagName === "SELECT")`. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image. Grepped the whole Playwright suite for anything touching `UserAutocomplete`'s old placeholder text, a bare `getByRole("combobox")` near a "Project groups"/"Organisation groups" tab, or a `getByRole("button", ...)` click on a dropdown match (now `role="option"`, not `"button"`, per the same WAI-ARIA pattern) before running anything — found and fixed the same class of break in `external-project-invite.spec.ts` (`getByRole("button", {name: /Invite .../})` → `getByRole("option", ...)`), `org-security-controls.spec.ts` and `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` (old placeholder text), and `org-group-nesting.spec.ts` + `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` (ambiguous `getByRole("combobox")`/`.last()` now also matching `UserAutocomplete`'s input on the same tab — scoped to `page.locator("select")` instead).

**A genuinely non-idempotent spec found and fixed along the way, per the user's explicit request** (this surfaced directly while chasing what looked like a regression): `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` created its four custom fields with fixed names ("Verification method", "Detailed rationale", "Safety critical", "Priority") on the shared seeded Beta-2 project. Custom Fields has no server-side uniqueness constraint on name, so re-running this spec against an already-mutated Beta-2 (no reseed in between — exactly what happened during this task's own iterative debugging of the locator fixes above) doesn't fail outright on the *first* assertion, but leaves every earlier run's own same-named fields behind, and a later step or a different spec sharing Beta-2 can trip over the resulting duplicates in a way that's hard to distinguish from a real regression (a "New field" button observed stuck disabled, initially misread as a possible UI bug before being traced back to this). Fixed by giving all four field names a shared per-run `Date.now()` suffix, threaded through every step that references them, removing the fixed-name assumption entirely — verified by running the spec three times back-to-back against the same unseeded database (no failures), where it previously failed on the second run onward. This is the concrete case the `CLAUDE.md` idempotency rule (added earlier this session) was written for; no other spec was found to need the same fix in this pass.

`docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done." Files changed: `frontend/src/components/UserAutocomplete.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/ProjectAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/external-project-invite.spec.ts`, `org-security-controls.spec.ts`, `org-group-nesting.spec.ts`, `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`.

## Unify the three "aggregate" list pages: Notifications, Favourites, My Reviews Due

Next M-effort roadmap item — the audit's "Pattern inconsistency" finding: Notifications, Favourites, and My Reviews Due are all "your stuff, across every project" list pages, and each presented that list differently — a clickable-card list with pagination, a card grid with none, and a plain table with neither filters nor pagination. My Reviews Due's project-scoped sibling page (`ProjectReviewsDuePage`) even has component/reviewer filters the cross-project version lacked entirely, on the same underlying data.

**Scoped to structure, not row content.** Favourites (projects), Notifications (notification events), and My Reviews Due (requirements) are three genuinely different entity types — forcing one literal shared row/card component across all three would be a worse fit than three separate, purpose-built renderings (the same reasoning `DefinitionList` was scoped away from stages/components earlier this session). What's actionable and was actually inconsistent: pagination shape, and whether cross-project rows show which project they belong to.

**Backend**:
- **`routers/projects.py::list_projects`**: new `favorite_only: bool = False` query param, filtering the already-built `out` list before the existing sort/count/slice — the same paginated `GET /projects` endpoint `ProjectListPage` uses, not a separate favourites endpoint, so `FavouritesPage` gets search and pagination for free rather than reimplementing them.
- **`routers/reviews.py::list_my_due_reviews`** (`GET /me/reviews/due`): gained the same optional `limit`/`offset`/`X-Total-Count` contract as `list_requirements` (omitting both still returns everything), and each row now carries `project_name` — resolved via the same batch-lookup-by-id pattern the endpoint already used for `component_name`. `RequirementDueForReviewOut.project_name` is optional and only ever populated here; the project-scoped `/projects/{id}/requirements/reviews/due` sibling leaves it `None`, since that page already has the project in its own URL and showing its own name back would be redundant.

**Frontend**:
- **`FavouritesPage.tsx`**: converted from `api.get` + client-side `.filter(p => p.is_favorite)` (an unbounded fetch of *every* accessible project just to find the favourited ones) to `api.getPage` against `/projects?favorite_only=true`, plus a search input — matching `ProjectListPage`'s and `NotificationsPage`'s own `PAGE_SIZE`/`total`/`LoadMoreButton` convention exactly.
- **`MyReviewsDuePage.tsx`**: same `api.getPage`/`LoadMoreButton` conversion, plus a new Project column using the backend's new `project_name` field.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. Backend: two new tests in `test_pagination.py` (`test_projects_list_favorite_only_filters_to_favourited_projects`, `test_my_reviews_due_paginates_reports_total_and_includes_project_name`) plus the existing `test_project_config.py`/`test_review_scheduling.py` suites re-run clean (the shared `RequirementDueForReviewOut` schema change is additive/optional, so its other consumer's behaviour is unchanged). Full backend suite: **535/535 passed** — rebuilt the backend image first (its source isn't bind-mounted; running new tests against the previous build's binary would have silently tested stale code), then ran via `docker compose exec backend python3 -m pytest tests/` — note `python3 -m pytest`, not a bare `pytest`, since this container's `tests/` has no `__init__.py` and a bare invocation left `/app` off `sys.path`, failing every test at collection with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'app'` before any actually ran. Frontend Storybook: `FavouritesPage.stories.tsx` and `MyReviewsDuePage.stories.tsx` both converted from `spyOn(api, "get")` to `spyOn(api, "getPage")`, with new `LoadMoreAppendsTheNextPage` stories on both and a new `SearchNarrowsTheList` story on Favourites; full suite 54 files / 368 tests, all green. Rebuilt/recreated both the `tests/container` backend and frontend images. Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and one full Playwright suite run: **64/64 passed** — no spec needed changes, since neither page's DOM contract (button roles/text, card/table shape) changed, only how the data behind it is fetched. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done." Files changed: `backend/app/routers/projects.py`, `backend/app/routers/reviews.py`, `backend/app/schemas/requirement.py`, `backend/tests/test_pagination.py`; `frontend/src/pages/FavouritesPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `MyReviewsDuePage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/api/types.ts`.

## Logo & login-background reset

Next M-effort roadmap item, and the audit's other "Platform defaults & overrides" finding: accent colour and (as of the previous "Reset affordance for text-based branding overrides" item) the header title/email-footer fields all have a working revert-to-platform-default path, but the org logo and login-background image had none at all — in the UI or the API. Upload-only, with no `DELETE` route to clear the override. Explicitly flagged in the roadmap as touching org branding and needing the change-management policy's identify → verify → remediate review before being considered complete.

**Backend**: two new endpoints in `routers/orgs.py`, `DELETE /{organization_id}/logo` and `DELETE /{organization_id}/login-background`, both `require_org_role(OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN)`-gated (the exact same dependency the sibling upload endpoints already use). Each is a genuine no-op (200, unchanged, no audit event) when nothing is currently set — reverting a setting already at its default isn't an error, it's asserting an end state that may already hold — and otherwise nulls the FK, deletes the now-orphaned `FileAsset` via the existing `services.files.delete_file` (removing both the storage bytes and the row, not just unlinking it — the same cleanup discipline `delete_org_resource` already uses), and writes a `logo_removed`/`login_background_removed` audit event via `log_event` before committing.

**Frontend**: `OverridePill` gained an optional `disabled` prop (previously only used by the batched-edit text fields, which have no in-flight state of their own to disable against). The org logo and login-background image fields (`OrgAdminPage.tsx`, Branding and Single sign-on sections respectively) now each show an `OverridePill` next to their label, wired to a new `removeLogo`/`removeLoginBackground` handler that calls the new `DELETE` endpoint and reloads immediately — unlike the text fields' reset (which just clears the input locally and waits for the section's own Save button), these fire and persist immediately, matching how the upload itself already commits with no separate Save step. Both show a toast on success/failure, extending the `Toast` pilot (previously on requirement archive, action archive, and branding save) to two more mutations.

**Identify → verify → remediate review** (`docs/soc2/policies/change-management-and-secure-development-policy.md`):
- *Identify*: (1) authorization — must be scoped to the acting org, not any org; (2) multi-tenant isolation — must only ever touch the calling org's own FK/asset, never another org's; (3) audit trail — a mutating action must not bypass `services/audit.py`'s logging pattern; (4) data retention — a reverted image shouldn't linger as an orphaned, unreferenced blob in storage indefinitely; (5) the public, unauthenticated `GET /api/v1/files/{id}` download route (which serves org branding images with no auth, by design, for the pre-login page) must not go on serving a logo/background after it's been reset.
- *Verify*: (1)/(2) confirmed by reusing `require_org_role(OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN)` unmodified — identical to `upload_org_logo`/`upload_login_background`, and it already resolves `organization_id` from the path, so there's no way to reach another org's row through this endpoint; `test_org_member_cannot_delete_org_logo` pins (1) directly. (3) confirmed by the new `log_event` calls, only fired on an actual mutation (the no-op path deliberately doesn't log a no-op as if it were a real deletion). (4) confirmed by calling `delete_file` rather than only clearing the FK — `test_delete_org_logo_and_login_background_revert_to_the_platform_default` pins this by asserting the old file's `GET /files/{id}` 404s afterward, not just that the org's FK went null. (5) is the same assertion — a 404 on the download route is exactly "stopped serving it." One accepted, non-remediated edge case: two concurrent `DELETE` calls against the same still-set logo could both read a non-null `logo_file_id` and both attempt `delete_file` on the same already-removed storage key, a benign race (no privilege escalation, no data exposure) that `delete_org_resource` already carries under the same conditions — not a new risk class introduced by this change, so left as-is rather than adding locking disproportionate to the actual exposure.
- *Remediate*: no findings required a code change beyond what's described above.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint` both clean. Backend: four new tests in `test_files.py` (`test_delete_org_logo_and_login_background_revert_to_the_platform_default`, `test_delete_org_logo_is_a_noop_when_nothing_is_set`, `test_org_member_cannot_delete_org_logo`, plus the pre-existing upload tests re-run alongside them); full backend suite **535/535 passed**, rebuilt-image-first, `python3 -m pytest`. Storybook: `OverridePill.stories.tsx` gained `CustomWithResetDisabledWhileSaving`; `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx` gained `BrandingSectionLogoAndLoginBackgroundReset` (resets both, asserts the `DELETE` call and the success toast for each); full suite 54 files / 370 tests, all green. Rebuilt/recreated both the `tests/container` backend and frontend images. New/extended Playwright coverage in the existing `org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts` (the spec the earlier text-field reset item created) rather than a new file — a second test alongside the existing one, covering upload-then-reset for both the logo and the login-background image, with `afterEach` extended to also `DELETE` both (a no-op when unset, so safe to run unconditionally) alongside its existing branding-field restore, keeping the spec idempotent per `CLAUDE.md`'s testing-idempotency rule; confirmed by running it three times back-to-back with no reseed in between, all passing. Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and one full Playwright suite run: **64/65 passed** — the one failure (`project-history.spec.ts`, unrelated to org branding) confirmed via an isolated rerun to pass cleanly alone, a pre-existing timing flake matching the same pattern seen earlier this session with `report-image-attachment.spec.ts`, not a regression. `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done." Files changed: `backend/app/routers/orgs.py`, `backend/tests/test_files.py`; `frontend/src/components/OverridePill.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`.

## Org Admin → resource-menu restructure, incl. splitting Advanced

Next L-effort roadmap item — the style guide's Principle 1 finding that Org Admin's 15 flat, all-collapsed `CollapsibleSection`s independently invented a different answer than Project Admin's 8-tab bar to the same "many settings, one page" problem. The style guide's own "Pattern: settings hierarchy" section already specifies the exact fix (resource-menu sub-pages, for a page with more than 5 groups opened rarely as a whole) and ships both the "before" (15 sections) and "after" (6 groups) diagrams to implement against, so this pass is a faithful migration against an already-agreed design, not a fresh design exercise.

**New shared component, not a page-local layout.** `ResourceMenu.tsx`: a left-hand vertical menu of route-addressable groups with a content pane on the right — modelled on `Tabs.tsx`'s WAI-ARIA-conscious keyboard support (arrow keys move focus and activate, `Home`/`End` jump to the ends) but deliberately *not* built on `Tabs`' `role="tablist"`/`"tab"` pattern, since each group here is a real URL segment, not client-only state: the menu is built from real `<Link>`s with `aria-current="page"` on the active one, and arrow-key movement calls `navigate()` (mirroring Tabs' own automatic-activation model, just adapted from internal state to routing) rather than only moving a highlighted-but-unselected item. Reuses the app's existing `.nav-link`/`.nav-link.active` classes for its items so the visual language matches the persistent nav rail exactly (Principle 4: one component per pattern), rather than inventing new styling for what's visually the same "list of links, one highlighted" shape.

**Routing**: `App.tsx`'s `/orgs/:orgId/admin` route became `/orgs/:orgId/admin/:group?` (react-router v6.30 supports optional path segments natively). Grepped every frontend call site that links to `/orgs/${orgId}/admin` before touching this (`Layout.tsx`'s nav rail — no, that's `/orgs`, not `/orgs/:id/admin` — `OrgListPage.tsx`'s auto-redirect and directory links, `PreferencesPage.tsx`'s "Manage organisation" link, `ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx`) and confirmed all of them use the bare path with no group segment, which still resolves (falls back to "overview") since the segment is optional — none needed updating.
