**Content migration matched the style guide's "after" diagram exactly — regrouped, nothing deleted or invented.** The unsectioned top content (org name/rename, export, leave) plus the rare-use `Import/merge` and `Shared resources` sections became **Overview** (the only group keeping `defaultCollapsed` on those two sub-sections, per the diagram's own "rare, opened almost never" framing). **People** = Users + Groups. **Projects & workflow** = the Projects management section (group/membership matrix) + Project statuses + Link types. **Branding & defaults** = Branding + Default project template. **Templates & reports** = Report Defaults + Report templates. **Integrations & security** = SSO + SCIM + the old Advanced section's contents, split by what they actually govern rather than kept as one catch-all: SMTP/email fields and the test-email block became their own "SMTP & email" card; 2FA/self-signup/external-user-policy became a "Security" card; PAT max-lifetime moved into the existing Personal Access Tokens section; the `sso_group_mappings` role-mapping list moved into the existing SSO section (an OIDC/SSO concept, not a generic "Advanced" one). Login-background moved out of the SSO section (where it sat for no reason connected to SSO/OIDC) into Branding, alongside the org logo, per the diagram's own "Branding — logo, colour, footer" leaf. The SMTP & email and Security cards still share **one** `saveAdvanced` button (placed at the bottom of the Security card) — both remain one underlying `OrgAdvancedSettings` PUT regardless of which card the fields visually live in now; adding a second save endpoint or button was explicitly avoided as unnecessary. Every section moving into a non-Overview group had its `defaultCollapsed` flipped from `true` to `false` (kept as a `CollapsibleSection`, still user-collapsible) — leaving them collapsed-by-default under a resource menu that already narrows each group to 2–5 items would have reinvented the audit's own "one optional block inside a single view, never the whole page" problem one level down, just moved from page-scope to group-scope.

**Scope exclusion, named rather than silently dropped**: the style guide's G6a note describes moving IdP-group-name mapping "from Groups" into SSO. That note is about the *per-group* `idp_synced_group_name` field (state `idpSyncEdits`, handler `saveIdpSync`), rendered inline in each group's own row inside the Groups section — a fundamentally different, harder change than the other moves in this pass: it would need a new consolidated "all groups' IdP mappings" table/view under SSO to actually make sense there, not just relocating the existing per-row inline field (which would read as orphaned, disconnected from the group list it's about, if moved as-is). That's disproportionate scope for an otherwise pure regrouping pass, so it was left exactly where it is, inside Groups (which itself moved into People as described above) — the same kind of explicit, reasoned exclusion this session's earlier `DefinitionList` item made for stages/components (documented rather than silently dropped).

**A real bug found and fixed mid-pass, not deferred**: `ResourceMenu`'s root element was originally styled `className="row resource-menu"`, reusing the app's shared `.row` flex utility class for its own layout needs. `.row` is also relied on throughout the Playwright suite as a scoping selector for "one specific list row" (e.g. `page.locator(".row", { hasText: someRowsOwnText })`). Since `ResourceMenu`'s wrapper spans the *entire* content pane, giving it the `.row` class made it the new outermost `.row` match in DOM order for any text anywhere in the selected group — silently hijacking `project-admin-templates-and-reports.spec.ts`'s unscoped `page.locator(".row", { hasText: revisedName }).first()` delete-button lookup (a real Playwright failure caught by the full-suite run, not found by inspection). Fixed by giving the wrapper its own dedicated `.resource-menu` CSS class instead, moving the flex layout there rather than composing it from `.row` + inline overrides — the kind of naming collision worth flagging for any future component that visually resembles a `.row`/`.stack` shape but wraps a whole page's content, not one row of it.

**Two more bugs found incidentally during full-suite verification, unrelated to Org Admin but fixed per `CLAUDE.md`'s fix-don't-defer rule rather than logged as out of scope**, both in `pelion-v2.spec.ts` / `ProjectListPage.tsx` — neither file is otherwise part of this roadmap item:
1. `pelion-v2.spec.ts`'s "New project" org-picker locator (`page.locator("select:has(option:text-is('Default Organization'))")`) was unscoped/page-wide, written when the bootstrap admin account belonged to exactly one org. It's since started belonging to two (org-creation via the API grants the creator membership, and both `seed_e2e_dataset.py` and `seed_demo_data.py` create orgs through this same admin account) — at which point `ProjectListPage`'s own "Organisation" `FilterPanel` select (added by an earlier, already-landed but still-uncommitted pass in this same working tree) *also* lists every org name as an option, including "Default Organization," making the locator ambiguous between the two selects. Fixed by scoping to the "New project" form card specifically. A second, related bug surfaced once the first was fixed: the picker-presence check (`orgPicker.count() > 0`) ran immediately after `.click()`, but `.count()` takes an immediate, non-retrying snapshot rather than auto-waiting — invisible with a single org (the check always legitimately returned 0 either way) but a real race once a picker can actually be present, since the form's own re-render hadn't necessarily finished. Fixed by waiting for the Name input to actually attach first. A third, compounding bug: even with the picker reliably found, `ProjectListPage`'s own org-fetch is async and separate from the form's unconditionally-rendered Name/Summary inputs — clicking "New project" too soon after navigating to `/projects` could still see zero orgs loaded (picker legitimately absent at that instant). Fixed by waiting for network idle before opening the form, both at initial login and after navigating back to `/projects` later in the same spec.
2. Once the org-picker fixes correctly routed both projects to the same org, a fourth, independent bug surfaced in the *next* step: "Software"'s own "add category" form's `Name` field silently reverted to empty between being filled and the "New category" button being clicked, leaving it permanently disabled. Root-caused via a captured accessibility-tree snapshot at the moment of failure: `Prefix` (filled second, closer to the click) still read `"PERF"`, but `Name` (filled first) had reverted to empty — confirming a *second*, later re-render reset it, not a first-fill failure. The spec's own existing comment already documents why: `ProjectAdminPage`'s post-mutation `reload()` fires 9 requests, "7 concurrently, then two more awaited *sequentially* afterwards" — and the spec's existing single `waitForLoadState("networkidle")` call can resolve in the brief gap between those two waves, not only after both, letting the test fill the form mid-cascade. Fixed with a second `waitForLoadState("networkidle")` immediately before the click, defensively re-filling only whichever field (if either) actually got reset rather than assuming which — confirmed stable across two consecutive isolated reruns plus the final full-suite run.

**Collapse-default story fixes weren't just click-removal — two needed real disambiguation**, since sections that used to be mutually exclusive (only one accordion open at a time before a user expanded a second) are now simultaneously mounted within the same resource-menu group: `GroupsSectionShowsMembers` asserted `getByText(/Alex Morgan/)` unscoped, which became ambiguous once Users and Groups (both now open by default under People) both render that name (Users' own table row, Groups' own member list) — scoped to the Engineering group's own row. `ProjectStatusesDeleteDisabledAtLastRow` asserted the "only one left, delete disabled" title unscoped, which became ambiguous once Project statuses and Link types (both open by default under Projects & workflow) each independently satisfy "exactly one row" in their respective mocked fixtures — scoped to the Active row specifically.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint .` both clean throughout (re-checked after every fix, not just once at the end). New `frontend/src/components/ResourceMenu.stories.tsx` (5 stories: click-switches-group with unmounted-not-hidden non-active content, only-the-active-group carries `aria-current`, arrow-key navigate-and-activate with wraparound, light/dark theme). Full Storybook suite: **55 files / 375 tests, all green** — `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s pre-existing ~25 stories were read individually (not run-then-reactively-patched) and updated to select the correct resource-menu group before asserting on content that's no longer reachable from a bare page load, with two of them needing the disambiguation described above beyond a simple group-selection swap. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image (twice — once for the initial page/component change, once more after the `.row`-collision CSS fix). Grepped the whole Playwright suite for Org Admin interactions before touching anything, beyond the files named in this task's own brief: found and fixed 7 spec files in total (`org-admin-project-statuses-and-link-types.spec.ts`, `org-group-nesting.spec.ts`, `org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`, `org-security-controls.spec.ts`, `org-rename-and-test-email.spec.ts`, `scim-provisioning.spec.ts`, `project-admin-templates-and-reports.spec.ts`), each via a new `selectOrgAdminGroup` helper added to `helpers.ts` (idempotent guard on `aria-current`, mirroring `ensureExpanded`'s existing idempotent guard on `aria-expanded`) — `org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`'s login-background step also lost its now-unnecessary SSO-section navigation entirely, since that field lives in the already-open Branding card now. Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and full Playwright suite runs: the first came back **62/65 passed**, all three failures root-caused rather than assumed — one was the `.row`-collision bug above (real regression, fixed), the other two (`project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`'s terminology-override step, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`) don't touch Org Admin or `ResourceMenu` at all and were confirmed via isolated reruns: the terminology one passed cleanly alone (a timing flake, left alone — neither of this session's two named pre-existing flakes, but the same class of issue, and not caused by this change); `pelion-v2.spec.ts` failed the *same way* alone too, which is what led to root-causing and fixing the three unrelated bugs described above rather than dismissing it as flaky. A second full clean-slate run came back **64/65** (only the by-then-already-diagnosed `pelion-v2.spec.ts` category-form race left, mid-fix). The **third and final full clean-slate run, after all fixes: 65/65 passed.**

`docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` roadmap Status updated to "Done," with the IdP-mapping scope exclusion named in the Notes column. Files changed: `frontend/src/components/ResourceMenu.tsx` (new, + `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/styles/theme.css`, `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/helpers.ts`, `org-admin-project-statuses-and-link-types.spec.ts`, `org-group-nesting.spec.ts`, `org-branding-override-reset.spec.ts`, `org-security-controls.spec.ts`, `org-rename-and-test-email.spec.ts`, `scim-provisioning.spec.ts`, `project-admin-templates-and-reports.spec.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/pelion-v2.spec.ts` (unrelated-bug fixes, per above).

## Project Admin's 8-tab bar → 5 tabs

Next L-effort roadmap item, picked because the two shared components its own roadmap row named as prerequisites (`CollapsibleSection`, already in wide use; `Popover`, built for Org Admin's own "New group" flow) already existed from earlier passes this session, and `DefinitionList` (the "Shared 'definition-table CRUD' component" item, Partial) already covers Action Types here — the remaining work was purely tab count/organisation, not new shared infrastructure. Unlike Org Admin's restructure, this is **not** a resource-menu conversion: the style guide's own decision tree routes "5 or fewer views, all relevant together" (Project Admin's tabs, actively used inline while administering a project) to `Tabs`, and only "more than 5 groups, opened rarely as a whole" to a resource menu — Project Admin's tabs are the former, just one over the ceiling, so this is a consolidation, not a pattern change.

**Exact grouping, 8 → 5:**
- **Overview** — unchanged (name/summary/visibility/status/archive/export), plus Terminology's own JSX/state/handlers relocated underneath as a pure move, no logic touched — a `<hr>` divider and its own (unchanged) `<h2>Terminology</h2>` sub-heading separate it from Overview's settings, per the task's "at minimum a sub-heading" floor rather than a `CollapsibleSection` (a bare relocation didn't need the extra accordion machinery, and terminology-override *architecture* — making `strings.ts` itself terminology-aware, the actual high-value fix — stays deferred to last per direct prior user instruction; this item only moves where its existing, still-mostly-inert UI lives).
- **Structure** — Stages + the Components/Categories tree, each its own `CollapsibleSection`, both `defaultCollapsed={false}` (a freshly-merged tab shouldn't also hide half its own content by default, same reasoning the Org Admin restructure used for sections moving into a non-Overview resource-menu group). Fixes the audit's "tab whose own heading disagreed with its label" finding: the tree's own `<h2>` used to read bare "Components" under a "Categories" tab; it's now the `CollapsibleSection`'s own title, "Components & categories" — accurate to what the block actually is (a two-level component→category tree), and the redundant inner `<h2>` was deleted rather than left duplicating the new section header.
- **Fields & Actions** — Custom Fields + Action Types (the latter already on `DefinitionList`), same two-`CollapsibleSection`, both-open shape.
- **Groups** — unchanged membership UI, plus a new "New group" `Popover` — the audit's "a 'New X' flow with no create form" finding, the one tab that broke the app's otherwise-universal "every X has an inline create form" pattern. Copied Org Admin's own "New group" popover shape exactly (trigger ref, `newGroupPopoverOpen` state, Enter-to-submit on the name field, Cancel/Create row, a `useToast()`-fired "Group created" success toast per Principle 7) with one real difference: `ProjectGroupCreate` requires `role: ProjectRole` up front (a project group's role can't be changed after creation — no update endpoint exists for it), so this popover has a second field, a role `<select>` (same four options/order as `ProjectListPage`'s own role filter) defaulting to `member`. No backend change needed — `POST /projects/{id}/groups` and its schema already supported this; verified by reading `backend/app/schemas/project.py::ProjectGroupCreate` and the router before assuming otherwise.
- **Reports** — unchanged content, two fixes named in the audit: (a) the tab's own `<h2>` was a literal `"Report Setup"` string, the one heading in the file not pulled from `strings.ts` (the tab bar's own label already was) — now `{strings.admin.reportSetup}`; (b) its four settings groups, never wrapped in anything despite being exactly `CollapsibleSection`'s "optional block within a view" case, are now two sections — "Default template" (the template-selector dropdown, only rendered when the org has templates) and "Report content" (intro + body chapters + appendices, kept together rather than split three ways, mirroring `OrgAdminPage`'s own analogous "Report Defaults" section which already bundles the same trio under one heading — treating three fields that share one Save handler and one subject as one group, not an arbitrary three-way split for its own sake). The one shared `saveReportConfig`/"Save settings" button stays outside both sections, unaffected by which is currently collapsed.

**Deliberately left alone, named rather than silently dropped:**
- The four delete/reassign behaviours (Custom Fields, Stages, Categories/Components, Action Types) — already a considered, documented scope decision from the `DefinitionList` item earlier this session (see that entry's own reasoning: Stages'/Components'/Categories' delete flows carry extra non-generic behaviour a shared component would force awkwardly). This item is about tab *count and organisation*, not that inconsistency.
- Terminology-override architecture (`strings.ts` becoming terminology-aware, all 6 terms wired up) — deferred to last per direct prior user instruction; untouched beyond relocating Terminology's existing, unchanged UI as described above.

**A real accessible-naming collision found and fixed, not just a test inconvenience**: merging Terminology under Overview put two buttons both labelled "Save settings" (Overview's own project-settings save, and Terminology's) on one screen — a genuine Principle 8 violation (a screen reader or keyboard user tabbing through hears "Save settings… Save settings" with no way to tell them apart), not only a `getByRole` test ambiguity, so the fix went in the component, not just the tests: Terminology's button is now labelled "Save terminology" (`strings.admin.saveTerminology`, new). This is a deliberate, flagged deviation from the task's "pure relocation of Terminology's JSX, nothing else" instruction — the collision the merge itself introduced wasn't something a pure JSX move could avoid, and fixing it is a one-line text change, not a logic/state/handler change. A second, same-shape collision surfaced in Reports: the new "Default report template" `CollapsibleSection`'s own `aria-label` ("Default report template section") is a substring of the field's own `<label>` text, and Playwright/Testing-Library's `getByLabel` matches substrings by default — `getByLabel("Default report template")` resolved to both the section header and the actual `<select>`. Fixed the same way, by giving the section a distinct title ("Default template," `strings.admin.defaultTemplateSection`) rather than reusing the field's own label text, leaving the field's own label (needed for its own accessible name) untouched.

**Pre-existing test fragility surfaced, not caused, by the merge — fixed rather than dismissed**: `golden-path.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, and `pelion-v2.spec.ts` each compute a "this component's own row" locator as `input[value="Software"]` (or `"Web"`) → `xpath=../../..`, with no guard against also matching the *bottom* "add component" form's own Name field, which transiently still holds the just-typed value for one render before `setNewComponentName("")` clears it — a real, if narrow-window, race already implicitly relied on being harmless. Before this merge it was: the ancestor climb from either match stayed within the (then-standalone) Categories tab's own root. After merging Stages into the same "Structure" tab, `CollapsibleSection` adds one more wrapping level, so the *same* climb from the bottom field's transient match now escapes all the way up to the shared tab panel and picks up Stages' own "Name" field too — a real strict-mode violation, reproduced consistently (not a flake) in `pelion-v2.spec.ts`. Fixed with the `:not([placeholder])` guard `project-admin-structural.spec.ts` already established for exactly this shape (only a genuine rename input for an existing row is ever placeholder-less) in all three specs.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint .` clean throughout (re-checked after every fix). Storybook: `ProjectAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s existing 17 stories read individually and updated for the new tab names/`CollapsibleSection` scoping (not run-then-reactively-patched), plus one new story, `GroupsTabCreateGroupViaPopover` (mirrors Org Admin's own popover-create story: open, type a name, pick a role, submit, assert the `POST .../groups` call with `{name, role}` and the "Group created" toast). Full suite: **55 files / 376 tests, all green**. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image (twice — once for the page/strings changes, once more after the two label-collision fixes). Grepped the whole Playwright suite for every old tab name (`Categories`, `Custom fields`, `Action types`, `Terminology`, `Project stages`) before considering this done, beyond the files anticipated in the task brief — found and fixed 9 spec files in total: `golden-path.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`, `project-admin-structural.spec.ts`, `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` (also gained the new "create group via popover" Playwright coverage, placed *after* the steps relying on "Members" being the last group in DOM order, since the new group would otherwise become the new last one and break those), `project-admin-action-types.spec.ts`, `project-admin-templates-and-reports.spec.ts` (no tab-name change needed — "Report Setup" kept its label — but exercised the "Default template" section-title fix), `stage-review-and-completion.spec.ts`, and `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` — the last two found only via the full-suite grep, not named in the task brief. Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and Playwright suite runs: the first came back 62/65 (the three failures above — the terminology-tab-navigation gap, the two label collisions surfacing as `getByLabel`/`getByRole` ambiguity, and the pre-existing `softwareRow`/`webRow` race — each root-caused rather than assumed, per `CLAUDE.md`'s fix-don't-defer rule); the **second, full clean-slate run after all fixes: 65/65 passed**, with neither of this session's two named pre-existing flakes (`report-image-attachment.spec.ts`, `project-history.spec.ts`) appearing. Backend: no backend change was needed (`POST /projects/{id}/groups` and `ProjectGroupCreate` already supported the new popover's payload as-is, confirmed by reading the schema/router before assuming otherwise) — the full backend suite was still re-run against the rebuilt image as a sanity check per the change-management policy's practice for changes touching an existing authorization-adjacent endpoint: **538/538 passed**, `python3 -m pytest tests/`.

**An unrelated but serious bug found incidentally while drafting this entry**: this file (`docs/decisions.md`) had lost essentially its entire history in the working tree — the uncommitted copy this task started from had been silently reduced to 26 lines (just the tail of the "Logo & login-background reset" entry, decapitated of its own `##` heading in the process, plus the following "Org Admin → resource-menu restructure" entry) — almost certainly an earlier pass in this same session overwriting the whole file with `Write` instead of appending with `Edit`. The agent that found this restored the file back to `git show HEAD:docs/decisions.md` (1354 lines) plus the two salvaged entries plus this one — but HEAD itself was already stale by three whole entries authored earlier in this same session and never committed ("Consolidate filter UI onto `FilterPanel`", "A visible 'Invite' entry point + keyboard navigation for `UserAutocomplete`", "Unify the three 'aggregate' list pages"), and its guessed heading for the decapitated entry ("Org branding: logo & login-background reset") wasn't the real one. Caught and fully corrected in the same session, from the original text still held in that session's own context: the three missing entries reinserted verbatim in their correct chronological position, and the decapitated entry's real heading ("Logo & login-background reset") and its full lost body (intro paragraph, Backend/Frontend sections, and the identify → verify → remediate review write-up — only the final "Verified" paragraph had survived) restored in full. The file's `## ` heading list now matches this session's actual work item-for-item, with no inferred or guessed content remaining.

Files changed: `frontend/src/pages/ProjectAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/golden-path.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`, `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/project-admin-structural.spec.ts`, `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`, `project-admin-action-types.spec.ts`, `stage-review-and-completion.spec.ts`, `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`; `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`; `docs/decisions.md` (this entry, plus fully restoring the prior history lost earlier in the session, per above).

## Terminology overrides now cover their own surface: `strings.ts` becomes terminology-aware

Last item on the 2026-08 UX audit roadmap (L effort, deferred to last per direct prior user instruction — see that roadmap row and the "Terminology overrides are a fixed, closed set of keys" entry above). The audit's finding: of C-C-03's six overridable nouns (project, stage, component, category, requirement, change_request), only 2 (requirement, change_request) were consumed anywhere, and even those only on their own list page + nav label — the requirement detail page, its comments, review-due lists, the CSV import wizard, and (most visibly) Project Admin's own custom-fields entity-kind dropdown all hardcoded the English word one tab over from the Terminology settings meant to rename it.

**Architecture.** `frontend/src/i18n/terminology.ts` (new): `TerminologyKey`, `DEFAULT_TERMS`, `pluralize()` (fixes a real bug — a naive `+s` produced "categorys"; now handles the consonant+y→ies rule), and `applyTerminology`/`resolveTerminologyDeep` — a `{token}` substitution mechanism recognising a fixed `TOKEN_MAP` (`{project}/{Project}/{projects}/{Projects}`, and the same 4-way pattern for stage/component/category/requirement/changeRequest). `strings.ts`'s existing string table gets these tokens embedded directly in its English values (e.g. `newRequirement: "New {requirement}"`) rather than converting to a wrapper-function-per-string approach — chosen because it's a minimal diff over the existing `t()`/`Strings` shape everything already imports, preserves every existing key/call-site name, and keeps the "one string table, looked up by key" mental model intact; a function-per-string approach would have required touching every one of the ~800 entries' *shape*, not just tagging the ~150 that are genuinely one of the six concepts. This doesn't contradict the earlier "fixed, closed set of keys" decision above — that decision was about *not* opening the key set to arbitrary nouns, not about whether consumption should be fully templated; the six-key set stays exactly as fixed as before. `TerminologyContext.tsx` gained `useStrings()`: returns `strings.ts`'s whole table with every token substituted for the current project's resolved terminology (memoized on the `ProjectContext` terminology object), the terminology-aware replacement for calling `t()` directly.

**Consolidating the old ad hoc mechanism.** Before this, 5 call sites in 3 files (`Layout.tsx`, `RequirementsPage.tsx`, `ChangeRequestsPage.tsx`, `ProjectOverviewPage.tsx`) bypassed `strings.ts` for the two "working" terms, computing them directly via `useTerm`/`useTermPlural` and interpolating raw into JSX (e.g. `<Plus/> New {requirementTerm}`). Now that the corresponding `strings.ts` entries are tokenized, this was a second, redundant mechanism for the same thing — consolidated onto `useStrings()`, the ad hoc local variables deleted. `useTerm`/`useTermPlural` remain exported for the one legitimate remaining use case: a raw resolved term needed outside a string template (e.g. `CsvImportWizard.tsx`'s in-component `FIELDS` array, built with `useMemo` since it can't be computed at module scope like before — see below), plus one bare-fallback-noun spot in `ChangeRequestsPage.tsx` with no natural `strings.ts` key to route through.

**Frontend call-site conversion.** The dominant existing pattern was `const strings = t();` at *module scope* — outside the component function, which is exactly why plain `t()` could never be terminology-aware (not even inside a render). Converted every page/component confirmed to render at least one tokenized key to `const strings = useStrings();` *inside* the component body: `ProjectAdminPage`, `RequirementDetailPage`, `ChangeRequestDetailPage`, `ProjectListPage`, `OrgAdminPage`, `ServerOrganisationsPage`, `ProjectReviewsDuePage`, `MyReviewsDuePage`, `PreferencesPage`, `ReportsPage`, `ActionDetailPage`, `ProjectHistoryPage`, `FavouritesPage`, `ImportConflictPanel`, plus the 4 ad hoc-mechanism files above. Files confirmed to use zero tokenized keys (`BrandingContext`, `ConfirmDialog`, `SubscribeButton`, `CommentThread`, `SidePanel`, `Modal`, `LoadMoreButton`, `UserAutocomplete`, `DefinitionList`, `LoginPage`, `OrgListPage`, `OrgLoginPage`, `OidcCompletePage`, `NotificationBell`, `NotificationsPage`, `SignupPage`, `ServerManagementPage`, `ProjectActionsPage`) were deliberately left on plain `t()` — verified independently with a Python script that parses `strings.ts`'s actual nested structure, extracts every key whose value contains a real `TOKEN_MAP` token (as opposed to the pre-existing `{org}`/`{name}`/`{n}`/etc. call-site placeholder convention, which uses the same brace syntax but isn't a terminology token), and cross-references every `strings.*.*` call site in every `.tsx`/`.ts` file against that set — confirming zero files render a tokenized key while still calling `t()` at module scope.

**Leaks outside `strings.ts` entirely — the highest-value fixes, since a mechanical `t()`→`useStrings()` sweep could never catch these:**
- `frontend/src/api/types.ts`'s `CUSTOM_FIELD_ENTITY_KIND_LABEL` — a plain module-level `Record`, which can never be terminology-aware since it's a static export, not a hook — the audit's single most visible example (Project Admin's own custom-fields entity-kind dropdown reading "Requirement"/"Change request" literally, one tab over from Terminology). Deleted; `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`'s existing-field badge now derives from `strings.admin.entityKindRequirement`/`entityKindChangeRequest` (already tokenized, already used by the "New field" dropdown) via a small inline ternary instead.
- `frontend/src/components/CsvImportWizard.tsx`'s module-level `FIELDS` array (`label: "Component"`, `label: "Category"` hardcoded) — restructured into a `buildFields(componentTerm, categoryTerm)` function called inside the component via `useMemo`/`useTerm`, since a module-level array can't call hooks. The two fields' *hint* text ("Must exactly match an existing component's prefix...") was also tokenized, the identical leak class one line below the label. The `level` field's hint (`'"requirement" or "recommended"...'`) was deliberately left untouched — it names the literal `RequirementLevel` wire values, not the terminology noun, and tokenizing it would be actively wrong. `buildTemplateCsv`'s column *keys* and the download filename (`requirements-import-template.csv`) stay literal machine identifiers, same reasoning as `component_prefix` being an internal identifier rather than display text — only the *displayed* mapping-UI labels needed to become term-aware.
- `services/reports.py::generate_csv_report()`'s hardcoded `["ID", "Name", "Component", "Category", ...]` CSV header — gained an optional `terminology: dict[str, str] | None` parameter; the two affected cells now come from `(terminology.get(key) or default).capitalize()`. Its only caller, `routers/reports.py::generate_csv()`, already had `project` in scope and now passes `project.terminology`. Grepped `services/reports.py` and `services/labels.py` in full for any other hardcoded terminology-noun display text — found none: the PDF report's own table header is `["ID", "Name", "Reasoning", "Status"]` (component/category names are rendered as chapter/section *headings*, never the literal word "Component"/"Category"), and `labels.py` only maps `RequirementStatus` enum values, not a terminology noun.

**Two further raw leaks found during verification, not named in the original task brief, fixed as the same class of bug (an existing tokenized `strings.ts` key that was simply never wired to this particular call site) rather than left for later:** `ProjectListPage.tsx`'s list-view table had a bare `<th>Stage</th>` never routed through `strings.ts` at all (now `{strings.projects.stage}`, "Current {stage}"); `ProjectReviewsDuePage.tsx` and `RequirementsPage.tsx` each had a `FilterField label="Component"`/`label="Category"` hardcoded despite the same file already using tokenized keys two lines away (now `strings.requirements.component`/`.category`, reusing the exact same `{Component}`/`{Category}` keys `RequirementsPage.tsx`'s own create-form labels already used). Found by grepping the whole frontend for bare `>Noun<` and `label="Noun"`/`title="Noun"` JSX patterns for all six nouns after the named files were done, rather than assuming the task brief's list was exhaustive.

**Flagged by the implementing agent for my own review rather than fixed unilaterally, then reviewed and fixed directly** (each needed a new key/token added to `strings.ts`, which the task brief reserved for me specifically: "do not re-tag or re-scan `strings.ts`... flag it explicitly... rather than silently adding more tokens"). I checked each against real surrounding code before adding anything:
- `Layout.tsx`'s `<div className="nav-section-label">Project</div>` was completely raw, zero `strings.ts` routing — a genuine leak the original tagging pass didn't reach, since it's a section label rather than an existing `nav.*` entry. Fixed with a new `nav.projectSectionLabel: "{Project}"` key. (Its sibling section labels "Global"/"Administration" are not terminology nouns at all and were correctly left alone — not part of this fix's scope.)
- `ProjectListPage.tsx`'s `<FilterField label="Stage status">` was similarly raw — `projects.allStages` ("All {stage} statuses") only covered the dropdown's own empty option, not the field's own label. Fixed with a new `projects.stageStatusLabel: "{Stage} status"` key.
- `RequirementsPage.tsx`'s `<option value="">All categories</option>` was raw despite the same file already using `strings.requirements.category` two lines above. Fixed with a new `requirements.allCategories: "All {categories}"` key (kept local to the `requirements` section rather than reusing the existing but unrelated-section `reports.allCategories`, matching this file's own established per-section key convention). Its sibling `<option value="">All versions</option>` on the "Target version" filter (bound to the `stage` entity under the hood, per `target_stage_id`) was deliberately left as plain English, same reasoning `strings.ts`'s own `targetVersion: "Target version"` entry was never tagged in the first place — "version" reads as a distinct, pre-existing UI-copy choice for this one field rather than the "stage" concept itself (the CSV wizard's `target_version` hint text treats it the same way), not a leak of the terminology system as designed.

All three fixes re-verified after adding: `npx tsc -b` clean, full Storybook suite still 55/380 green (no story asserted the old raw text in a way that would break), full clean-slate Playwright suite (`down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts, `npx playwright test` unfiltered) **66/66 passed** again, including `terminology-coverage.spec.ts` and both named pre-existing flakes absent.

**Seed data.** Neither seed script set any project's `terminology` field before this (checked, zero hits) — per CLAUDE.md, a feature with no seeded demo value reads as absent. `seed_demo_data.py`: new `set_project_terminology()` helper, applied `{"stage": "Phase", "requirement": "Spec", "change_request": "ECR"}` to the Falcon-3 Inspection Drone project (aerospace-engineering vocabulary reads naturally for a hardware programme; deliberately partial — project/component/category left at defaults — to also demonstrate overrides don't have to be all-or-nothing). `seed_e2e_dataset.py`: rather than mutate one of the 6 existing named projects (all reused across many other specs, several of which assert exact default-English nav/button text that an override would break), added a 7th, dedicated project (`Delta-1 Terminology Demo`, `TERMINOLOGY_PROJECT_NAME`/`TERMINOLOGY_OVERRIDE` constants, same fixed override) reserved solely for the new terminology spec — documented in the script and in `docs/e2e-workflows.md`'s persona/project table and a new numbered workflow entry (#23).

**Tests.**
- *Storybook*: existing suite re-verified unchanged under default terminology first (a token with no override resolves to the same English word — any change would have signalled a wiring mistake, not a story needing an update). New stories using the existing `withTerminology()` decorator: `RequirementDetailPage.stories.tsx`'s `TerminologyOverrideAppliesThroughoutPage` (the "Make a change request" link and the archive-confirm dialog title); `ProjectAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s `CustomFieldsTabEntityKindDropdownReflectsTerminology` (the fix's headline example — both the existing-field badge and the "New field" dropdown's two options) and `StructureTabPluralisesCategoryCorrectly` (proves the irregular-plural fix against *default* terminology — "Components & categories", not "& categorys" — deliberately with no `withTerminology` decorator, since the bug being guarded is in the default-term pluralisation path itself); `ProjectReviewsDuePage.stories.tsx`'s `TerminologyOverrideAppliesToHeadingAndFilters` (the review-due heading + component filter column/option).
- *Playwright*: `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts` already exercised the terminology *save flow* itself (set "requirement"→"Spec" on Beta-2, confirm the nav relabels, revert) — left untouched per the task's explicit "don't touch the save flow" boundary. Added a new, separate spec, `terminology-coverage.spec.ts`, using the dedicated Delta-1 project (no set/revert dance needed, since its override is permanent and no other spec depends on Delta-1's terminology staying at any particular value): nav rail ("Specs"/"ECRs"), the requirement detail page's "Make ECR" link, Project Admin's custom-fields entity-kind dropdown (default "Spec", switched to confirm "ECR"), and the review-due list heading ("Specs due for review").

**Scope boundaries respected**: `TERMINOLOGY_KEYS` (backend, fixed/closed set) untouched; the terminology *save* UI/flow (Project Admin's Terminology tab, `PUT /projects/{id}/terminology`) untouched; `strings.ts` itself not re-scanned/re-tagged beyond what was already tagged going in.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint .` clean throughout (0 errors; re-checked after every batch of changes; the 5 pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings in `BrandingContext.tsx`/`ThemeContext.tsx` are unrelated and untouched). One genuine new lint finding fixed, not suppressed: converting `ProjectOverviewPage.tsx` to `useStrings()` surfaced a real `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning on its data-loading `useEffect` (it closes over `strings.common.error`, which is now a reactive value rather than a module constant) — added to the effect's dependency array rather than disabled inline. Storybook: **55 files / 380 tests, all green**. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` backend and frontend images before any live verification (neither bind-mounts source). Backend: pytest run first per the change-management policy's sequencing rule (never concurrent with Playwright) — **541/541 passed** (538 pre-existing + 3 new: `generate_csv_report`'s override/default-fallback behaviour, plus a router-level test proving `generate_csv`'s endpoint actually passes the project's *persisted* terminology through, not just that the service function accepts the parameter in isolation), `python3 -m pytest tests/`. Backend container restarted (pytest truncates all tables) and both seed scripts re-run before Playwright, per the standard sequence. Playwright: full clean-slate suite, no filter — **66/66 passed** (65 pre-existing + 1 new, `terminology-coverage.spec.ts`), including neither of this session's two named pre-existing flakes (`report-image-attachment.spec.ts`, `project-history.spec.ts`) appearing. Also grepped the whole Playwright suite for any spec asserting an exact default-English word on a project whose terminology this change touched — none found, since Delta-1 is dedicated and new, and Beta-2's terminology (used by `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`) was already reset to default at that spec's own end before this change and remains untouched by anything here.

Files changed: `frontend/src/i18n/terminology.ts` (new), `frontend/src/context/TerminologyContext.tsx`, `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts` (tokenized ahead of this implementation pass, plus 3 more keys added afterward for the flagged-and-fixed leaks above); `frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx`, `CsvImportWizard.tsx`, `ImportConflictPanel.tsx`; `frontend/src/pages/{RequirementsPage,ChangeRequestsPage,ProjectOverviewPage,ProjectAdminPage,RequirementDetailPage,ChangeRequestDetailPage,ProjectListPage,OrgAdminPage,ServerOrganisationsPage,ProjectReviewsDuePage,MyReviewsDuePage,PreferencesPage,ReportsPage,ActionDetailPage,ProjectHistoryPage,FavouritesPage}.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx` for `RequirementDetailPage`, `ProjectAdminPage`, `ProjectReviewsDuePage`); `frontend/src/api/types.ts`; `backend/app/services/reports.py`, `backend/app/routers/reports.py`, `backend/tests/test_reports.py`; `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`, `backend/scripts/seed_e2e_dataset.py`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/helpers.ts` (+ new `terminology-coverage.spec.ts`); `docs/e2e-workflows.md`, `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).

## First-pass UX review: "My organisations" leaked server-admin scope, no build-identity surface, resource menus with no title

Three issues from the user's own first-pass review of this session's UX-audit work, not roadmap items — logged here per this project's own convention of recording implementation decisions regardless of where the request originated.

**"My organisations" showed every org on the server to a server admin.** `OrgListPage.tsx` (the nav rail's "My organisations" link, `/orgs`) called the bare `GET /orgs`, which deliberately returns every org on the deployment for a server admin — I-M-05's platform-wide bypass, correct for `ServerOrganisationsPage`'s own directory but wrong here: this is a *personal* membership list, the same distinction `PreferencesPage.tsx`'s own "Access" tab had already worked around with an N+1 membership-probe (its own code comment names the exact same bug). The backend already had the right tool for this, unused by this one call site: `GET /orgs?mine=true`, an explicit opt-out of the server-admin bypass added for `ProjectListPage`'s org filter and already documented in `orgs.py::list_organizations`'s own docstring as existing for exactly this "organisations I can actually act within" case. Fixed with a one-line switch to that query param — no backend change needed, the endpoint already supported this.

*A real regression found during full-suite verification, not shipped silently*: `server-admin-scope.spec.ts` navigated to `/orgs` directly (`page.goto`, not the nav link) specifically to exploit the old unfiltered behaviour, reaching Alpha's degraded "not a member" admin view (I-M-05's other carve-out: a server admin can join/bootstrap any org's initial admin) via a page that was never the intended path for that — `ServerOrganisationsPage.tsx` already has its own "Edit" link straight to `/orgs/{id}/admin` per row, the real nav-linked route for this. The `mine=true` fix correctly broke the test's incidental shortcut without removing any real capability. Fixed by retargeting that test step through `/server/organisations` → its own "Edit" link, scoped to Alpha's row (`getByRole("row", { name: new RegExp(ORG_NAMES.alpha) })`, the same scoping pattern `server-org-lifecycle.spec.ts` already uses) — the test's stale comment ("/orgs isn't linked from the nav any more") was also inaccurate against current `Layout.tsx` (the "My organisations" link renders for every user, server admin included) and is no longer present now that the step doesn't depend on it.

**No way to see the running frontend/backend build version from the UI.** CI (`docker-build` job) already computes a GitVersion SemVer and tags every image with it plus the commit SHA, but nothing baked that identity *into* the image — a running container had no way to report its own version, only the tag it happened to be pulled under. Fixed by threading `APP_VERSION`/`GIT_SHA`/`BUILD_DATE` through as Docker build ARGs (both `backend/Dockerfile` and `frontend/Dockerfile`, defaulting to `dev`/`unknown` for a plain local build with no `--build-arg`) and wiring the CI job's existing `steps.gitversion.outputs.semVer`/`github.sha` (plus one new `Determine build date` step) into `build-args:` on both image builds. Backend: `app/version.py` reads them from the environment once at import time; new `GET /api/v1/system/version` (any authenticated user, not server-admin-gated — build metadata, not org content, same reasoning `/health` already uses for staying unauthenticated) returns them. Frontend: Vite's own `import.meta.env.VITE_*` build-time substitution picks up the same values (set as `VITE_`-prefixed `ENV` in the Dockerfile, distinct from `VITE_API_BASE_URL`'s *runtime* injection via `env-config-entrypoint.sh` — version is a property of the image itself, not the deployment target, so it belongs with Vite's build-time constants, not that mechanism) via a new `frontend/src/version.ts`. Surfaced as a small always-visible footer at the bottom of the nav rail ("App vX.Y.Z" / "API vX.Y.Z", full commit+build-date in each line's `title`), hidden when the rail is collapsed to icons-only like every other rail label — not a new Preferences tab, since Preferences already sits at the style guide's own 5-tab ceiling (Principle 1) and this is glanceable status, not a setting.

**The new resource menu had no title showing which org/project it was editing.** Two related but separate gaps, both from the audit's own earlier restructure passes: Org Admin's resource-menu conversion (see "Org Admin -> resource-menu restructure" above) moved the organisation's own `<h1>{org.name}</h1>` into the Overview group's content specifically — every other group (People, Projects & workflow, Branding & defaults, Templates & reports, Integrations & security) had no page title at all once you navigated off Overview, since `ResourceMenu` itself rendered nothing but the menu+content grid. Project Admin's 8-tabs-to-5 consolidation left its own `<h1>` as the generic `strings.nav.admin` ("{Project} admin") label, no project name anywhere on the page. Fixed by giving `ResourceMenu` an optional `title`/`subtitle`, rendered once above the menu+content grid so it persists across every group switch (unlike the per-group content it wraps) — `OrgAdminPage` passes `org.name` + "{OrgCap} admin" as subtitle, and the now-redundant duplicate `<h1>{org.name}</h1>` inside Overview's own content was removed rather than left duplicating text the page now shows universally. `ProjectAdminPage` (not a `ResourceMenu` consumer — it's `Tabs`, per the style guide's own 5-or-fewer-views routing) got the same fix directly: its `<h1>` now reads the project's own name, with the old generic label demoted to a muted subtitle underneath, matching `ProjectOverviewPage`'s own established `<h1>{project.name}</h1>` pattern elsewhere in the app.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint .` clean throughout (0 errors; the same 5 pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings, unrelated and untouched). Storybook: **55 files / 382 tests, all green** (new: `ResourceMenu.stories.tsx`'s `ShowsTitleAndSubtitle`, proving the title persists across a group switch; `Layout.stories.tsx`'s `ShowsVersionFooter`; `OrgListPage.stories.tsx`'s three existing stories tightened to assert the exact `mine=true` path rather than accepting any `api.get` call, so a regression back to the bare endpoint fails the story instead of silently passing). Backend: **543/543 passed** (541 pre-existing + 2 new, `test_system_version.py` — reachable by any authenticated user, 401 unauthenticated), `python -m pytest tests/ -q`. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` backend and frontend images before any live verification (neither bind-mounts source; confirmed both build cleanly with no `--build-arg`s, reporting the documented `dev`/`unknown` placeholders). Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, both seed scripts) and Playwright: first run came back 69/70 (the `server-admin-scope.spec.ts` regression above, root-caused rather than assumed, then fixed); **second full clean-slate run after the fix: 70/70 passed** (68 pre-existing + 2 new: `admin-page-titles-and-version-footer.spec.ts`, and one new test added to `org-admin-nav-link.spec.ts` proving the zero-membership server-admin persona sees the empty state), including neither of this session's two named pre-existing flakes (`report-image-attachment.spec.ts`, `project-history.spec.ts`) appearing.

Files changed: `frontend/src/pages/OrgListPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`); `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `backend/Dockerfile`, `frontend/Dockerfile`; `backend/app/version.py` (new), `backend/app/routers/system.py`, `backend/tests/test_system_version.py` (new); `frontend/src/version.ts` (new), `frontend/src/vite-env.d.ts`, `frontend/src/api/types.ts`, `frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `frontend/src/components/ResourceMenu.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/ProjectAdminPage.tsx`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/org-admin-nav-link.spec.ts`, `server-admin-scope.spec.ts`, `admin-page-titles-and-version-footer.spec.ts` (new); `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/e2e-workflows.md`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).

**A CI-only failure, root-caused rather than assumed.** The PR built on this work (`main...feature/ui-improvements`) failed CI's `Backend tests, coverage & E2E` job on `terminology-coverage.spec.ts`, which had passed on every local run in this session (including this entry's own full clean-slate re-verification). The actual failure: a strict-mode violation on `page.getByText("Must respond to input within 50ms")` — Playwright's default substring match is case-insensitive, and HW-FN-001's own seeded `reasoning` field restates its name in lowercase prose ("Reasoning: must respond to input within 50ms."), so the bare `getByText` resolved to both that paragraph and the actual requirement link, non-deterministically depending on render/viewport timing (present in CI's run, absent in every local run here). Fixed by scoping to `getByRole("link", { name: "Must respond to input within 50ms" })`, the only one of the two that's actually the intended, clickable target — re-verified locally (this spec alone, then a full clean-slate 70/70 run) before pushing.

## PR #9 CI run hung 18+ minutes with no output, had to be manually cancelled — no timeout anywhere in the workflow, and no ceiling on it

[Run 32209526768](https://github.com/ballardr/reqtrackmanager/actions/runs/32209526768), `backend-and-e2e`'s "Install Playwright browsers" step (`npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, `tests/playwright`), hung with zero output from `02:54:52Z` to `03:12:45Z` — user reported it as "stuck," cancelled it manually, and asked whether something needed fixing rather than assuming a fluke. Root-caused from the raw job log (`gh run view --job=<id> --log`), not assumed: the step's own `npm ci` and initial `apt-get update` output ran normally through `Get:5 https://archive.ubuntu.com/ubuntu noble-security InRelease`, then produced nothing further until the cancellation — a stall inside `apt-get update` against `archive.ubuntu.com`/`security.ubuntu.com`, a known flaky/slow-mirror issue on GitHub-hosted Ubuntu runners (whose apt sources mix the fast in-datacenter `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` mirror with these slower direct-to-Canonical ones), not a code or dependency regression. The real gap the incident exposed: `ci.yml` had no `timeout-minutes` anywhere — no step, no job — so a stall like this had nothing to stop it short of GitHub's 6-hour default job ceiling, and the cascading failures the user saw after cancelling (`docker-build` failing, "No test report files were found," missing `playwright-report.xml`) were all just downstream consequences of the cancellation (`docker-build` has `needs: [frontend, backend-and-e2e]`), not separate bugs.

Separately confirmed via each job's `startedAt` (`gh run view --json jobs`) that `frontend` and `backend-and-e2e` already start within the same second on every run checked, both this one and prior successful runs — the two jobs have no `needs:` between them and already run in parallel exactly as intended; the visible duration gap (frontend ~2 min vs. backend-and-e2e ~15-20 min normally) is inherent to backend-and-e2e's much longer serial step chain (Docker Compose build, two health-waits, pytest, two ruff lints, MCP tests, reseed, full Playwright suite), not a scheduling problem. No change made here.

**Fix**: `timeout-minutes` added to all four jobs (`frontend`: 15, `backend-and-e2e`: 45, `publish-badges`: 10, `docker-build`: 20 — each roughly 2-3x its normal observed duration) plus `timeout-minutes: 5` on both "Install Playwright browsers" steps specifically (frontend's and backend-and-e2e's), so a repeat of this mirror stall fails in 5 minutes instead of running until manually noticed and cancelled. Alongside this, both jobs' Playwright browser caches (`~/.cache/ms-playwright`, keyed on `${{ runner.os }}-playwright-${{ hashFiles(<that job's package-lock.json>) }}` — the two jobs pin different Playwright versions, `frontend/package.json`'s `playwright` vs. `tests/playwright/package.json`'s `@playwright/test`, so each needs its own key) are now cached via `actions/cache@v4`, cutting the ~300MB Chromium re-download on every run; this doesn't remove the `apt-get update` exposure itself (`--with-deps` runs it regardless of whether the browser binary was already cached, since OS-level packages don't persist across the fresh VM each job gets), which is what the timeout is for.

**Verified**: `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml'))"` clean. Not re-run against a live GitHub Actions run as part of this change (no code path to reproduce the mirror stall on demand) — the timeout values were sized against this run's own real step/job durations and prior successful runs' `gh run view --json jobs` timings, not guessed.

Files changed: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).

## UX audit, fifth pass: `FilterBadge` and grouped downloads written up as rules, entity lists at scale, "view a user" gap, and a real bug fix (Requirements' create form never actually reached `SidePanel`)

Requested as a fresh top-to-bottom UI review from the perspective of a UX design team lead, rather than a specific bug report — re-checked every page against the style guide the first four passes produced (not against the pre-audit UI), which surfaced gaps the earlier passes' own roadmap items had left half-closed. Full findings and roadmap in `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`'s new "fifth pass" intro paragraph and the sections it links (`#directories-at-scale-users-and-groups`, `#no-way-to-view-a-users-access`, `#badges-as-filter-shortcuts-real-but-not-everywhere`); two new style guide principles (10, 11) and two new pattern sections ("directories at scale," "entity detail panel") in `docs/ux-style-guide.md`. Per this project's own fix-don't-defer convention, three of the findings were fixed in the same pass rather than left on the roadmap:

**Requirement single-create converted onto `SidePanel` (a real bug, not just a style-guide gap).** The "One entry point for single vs. bulk requirement creation" item (this log, above) built the Popover chooser ("Add one"/"Import from CSV") but left "Add one" opening the same pre-audit inline card it always had — `SidePanel` existed (built for the fourth pass) but had never actually been wired to a real create flow anywhere in the app. Project Overview's own "+ New Requirement" link deep-links to `?new=1`, which set `showNewForm` directly, skipping the Popover — so from Overview the (identical, still-unconverted) form read as "a different, older form" than the one reached via the Popover on the Requirements page itself. Fixed by moving both branches of the create form (the main fields, and the "no components/categories yet" quick-setup variant) into `SidePanel`; both entry points now open the literal same layer, and the list underneath stays visible and untouched while it's open. `RequirementsPage.tsx`: two-column field rows (`component`/`category`, `target version`/`level`) collapsed to single-column `<label>` stacks to fit `SidePanel`'s 420px width. No behaviour change to any handler — `createRequirement`/`createInlineComponent`/`createInlineCategory` and their state are untouched, only the JSX container around them moved.

**CSV "Export CSV"/"Download template" combined behind one "Export" `Popover`.** Same shape as Principle 5's "one door for add one and add many," just for two downloads instead of a create/import pair — the style guide didn't say the rule extended past creates, so nothing flagged it. `CsvImportWizard.tsx` gained `exportMenuOpen`/`exportTriggerRef` state and one "Export" trigger button opening a `Popover` with the two existing handlers (`exportCsv`, the template-download `onClick`) unchanged, just relocated. Import CSV's own trigger/location (`showImportTrigger`) is untouched.

**`ProjectActionsPage`'s outcome badge wired to `FilterBadge`.** Its results table showed `outcome_status` as a plain `<span className="badge">`, one column over from a `FilterPanel` `<select>` driven by the identical value — `FilterBadge` (built fourth pass, already correct on `RequirementsPage`/`ChangeRequestsPage`/`ProjectListPage`/`ServerOrganisationsPage`) had never been written up as a rule, so a fifth page reproduced the gap it should have prevented. New `toggleOutcomeFilter` helper, same shape as the other four pages' own toggle functions.

**Deliberately left alone, on the roadmap, not fixed here:** search + pagination for Org Admin's Users/Groups and Project Admin's Groups tab, and a read-only "view user" detail panel — both real M-effort features (a new search param + `limit`/`offset` + `LoadMoreButton` wiring for the former; a new `SidePanel`-based read-only view, possibly a new aggregate read endpoint, for the latter), not S-effort UI-only fixes like the three above. Named explicitly in the audit roadmap rather than silently dropped.

**A non-idempotent test found and fixed while verifying the `SidePanel` change, per this repo's own convention (not deferred):** `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` archived the seeded requirement "Must log all state transitions" and recreated it under a derived name — not reversible from the UI, so a second run against the same database (which is exactly what happened verifying this change, before a deliberate clean-slate reseed made the gap visible) failed at the step expecting the original seeded name to still exist, unarchived. Fixed by having the test create its own dynamically-named throwaway requirement (`E2E Bypass Target ${Date.now()}`) at the start of the run instead of depending on the fixed seeded name surviving from a prior run; re-verified by running the spec twice back-to-back against the same database with no reseed in between.

**A second, unrelated locator fix, same root cause, surfaced by the `SidePanel` change itself:** `review-scheduling.spec.ts` and `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` both used an unscoped `page.getByRole("combobox").first()` immediately after opening the requirement create form, relying on it being the first `<select>` in DOM order. Once the form became a `SidePanel` (portalled to the end of `document.body`), the filter sidebar's own Status `<select>` — still inline, earlier in the page — became the new "first" combobox instead. Fixed by scoping both to `page.getByRole("dialog", { name: "New Requirement" })` first. Checked every other `getByRole("combobox").first()` in the suite (`golden-path.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, `change-request-tasks-and-voting.spec.ts`, `change-request-approval-separation.spec.ts`, `project-visibility.spec.ts`, `multi-org-admin.spec.ts`, `terminology-coverage.spec.ts`) — none of the others target the Requirements create form, so none needed the same fix; confirmed by running each affected file.

**Verified**: `npx tsc -b` and a full-tree `npx eslint .` both clean. Storybook (`vitest run --project=storybook`) on the three changed story files: 25/25 passed, including new stories `ExportMenuOffersCsvAndTemplate` (`CsvImportWizard.stories.tsx`) and `FilterByOutcomeBadge` (`ProjectActionsPage.stories.tsx`), and the existing `RequirementsPage.stories.tsx` create-flow stories updated to query `within(document.body)` for the now-portalled panel. Rebuilt/recreated the `tests/container` frontend image. Full clean-slate reseed (`docker compose down -v && up --build -d`, `seed_e2e_dataset.py`) before final verification, specifically to rule out the same "reused a mutated seeded fixture" false failures a mid-session non-clean rerun surfaced (and which led to the `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` fix above). Playwright, all currently green against the fresh dataset: `golden-path.spec.ts`, `mockup-engagement.spec.ts`, `pelion-v2.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/requirement-archive-confirm.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/review-scheduling.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts` (run twice back-to-back, no reseed between, to confirm the idempotency fix), `e2e-workflows/csv-import-wizard.spec.ts`, `e2e-workflows/project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`, and the new `e2e-workflows/overview-new-requirement-side-panel.spec.ts` (deep-links from Project Overview, asserts the `SidePanel` dialog and the untouched list are both visible at once, creates a requirement through it). Not run: a full-suite pass across every spec in `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/` — the targeted set above covers every spec that actually interacts with the requirement create form, CSV export/template, or `ProjectActionsPage`'s outcome badge, confirmed by grepping the whole suite for each of those three surfaces before deciding the targeted set was complete, rather than run-everything-and-see.

Files changed: `frontend/src/pages/RequirementsPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/components/CsvImportWizard.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/ProjectActionsPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`); `tests/playwright/tests/golden-path.spec.ts`, `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/csv-import-wizard.spec.ts`, `review-scheduling.spec.ts`, `workflow-bypass-attempts.spec.ts`, and new `overview-new-requirement-side-panel.spec.ts`; `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`, `docs/ux-style-guide.md`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).

## Search + pagination for Org Admin's Users/Groups and Project Admin's Groups tab

Sixth-pass roadmap item — the audit's own "Directories at scale" finding: at real scale (the user's own example: ~100 groups, ~1,000 users) these three admin lists had no search and no pagination, and both group lists rendered every group's full member list expanded, unconditionally, all the time.

**Backend, three endpoints, same contract as `list_requirements`**: `list_org_users` (`routers/orgs.py`), `list_org_groups`, and `list_project_groups` (`routers/projects.py`) each gained optional `search` (name/email substring, case-insensitive) and `limit`/`offset`, with the pre-slice total returned via `X-Total-Count`. Omitting `limit` still returns every match, unpaginated — deliberately, since existing unfiltered callers (Project Admin's own org-group nesting picker, calling `GET /orgs/{id}/groups` with no query params at all) needed to keep working unchanged. `list_org_users`' `search`/`limit`/`offset` are open to any caller who can already reach the endpoint, unlike the existing access-review filters (`stale_since_days`, `has_2fa`, etc.), which stay org-admin-gated — narrowing the same directory a plain member can already browse isn't an access-review signal. Added a stable `display_name`/`name` sort to all three (previously arbitrary dict/query order), required for offset-based pagination to actually behave — without it, "page 2" isn't a well-defined concept.

**Frontend — `OrgAdminPage.tsx` (Users and Groups sections)**: a search box + `LoadMoreButton` on Users, wired through a rebuilt `loadUsers(filter, search, offset, append)` (previously `loadUsers(filter)`, a single unpaginated `api.get`). Groups got the same search + `LoadMoreButton`, plus a real interaction-model change: each group now renders behind its own `CollapsibleSection` (`variant="plain"`, `defaultCollapsed`, title = `"<name> (<member count>)"`), collapsed by default, instead of an always-expanded `<div>`. One genuine complication, not just a rename: Org Groups can nest inside each other, and the nested-group name resolution (`nestedGroups`) and the "add nested group" `<select>`'s option list (`nestableGroups`) both need *every* group in the org, not whatever the Groups section's own search/page currently shows — resolving them against the paginated/searched `groups` state would silently drop nesting candidates that fall outside the current search term or page. Fixed with a second, deliberately-unpaginated `allGroups` fetch (the same `GET /orgs/{id}/groups` call with no query params, now doubling as this) — `groups` drives which groups render as cards, `allGroups` drives nesting resolution, kept as two separate pieces of state rather than one.

**Frontend — `ProjectAdminPage.tsx` (Groups tab)**: same search + `LoadMoreButton` + `CollapsibleSection`-per-group treatment, pulled out of the tab's big `reload()` `Promise.all` bundle into its own `loadGroups`. No `allGroups`-equivalent needed here — checked first, and confirmed a project group only ever nests *org* groups (resolved from the page's existing, separate, still-unpaginated `orgGroups` fetch), never another project group, so there's no self-referential nesting concern the way Org Admin's own groups have.

**A non-obvious a11y consequence, checked rather than assumed**: `CollapsibleSection`'s own docs already flag that a non-string `title` gets no `aria-label` fallback — this change is the first caller to actually pass a *computed* string (`` `${g.name} (...)` ``) rather than a static one, so it was worth confirming it stays a plain string (not JSX) specifically to avoid tripping that known, previously-dormant gap; it does.

**Verified**: New backend tests in `tests/test_pagination.py` (`test_org_users_search_and_paginate`, `test_org_groups_search_and_paginate`, `test_project_groups_search_and_paginate`) — pagination, total count, and search-by-name/email, plus (for groups) confirming an unpaginated call still returns everything. Full backend suite: 546/546 passed. `ruff check` clean (the pre-existing `ruff format` drift across 121 unrelated files, including these two routers, predates this change and wasn't introduced by it — not touched). `npx tsc -b` and `npx eslint .` both clean. Full Storybook suite: 55 files / 384 tests passed, including `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s and `ProjectAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s own group-section stories, updated for the new collapsed-by-default `CollapsibleSection` shape (`GroupsSectionShowsMembers`/`GroupsSectionNestGroup`/`GroupsTabAddMember`/`GroupsTabAddOrgGroup` each now expand the relevant group's card before asserting on its contents) and a new `spyOn(api, "getPage")` mock branch for `/users`/`/groups` in both files' shared mock helpers. Rebuilt/recreated both `tests/container` images; full clean-slate reseed before Playwright verification. New shared helper `openGroupCard()` (`tests/e2e-workflows/helpers.ts`, same idempotent-only-click-if-collapsed shape as the existing `ensureExpanded`) — four existing specs relying on a group's member list/add-member input/nesting picker being reachable without expanding anything (`org-group-nesting.spec.ts`, `org-security-controls.spec.ts`, `external-project-invite.spec.ts`, `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`) updated to call it first; all four, plus nine further admin-surface specs identified by grep as plausibly touching these sections (`multi-org-admin`, `org-admin-nav-link`, `admin-page-titles-and-version-footer`, `single-org-admin`, `user-directory-and-bans`, `server-admin-scope`), pass. Not run: a full-suite pass across every remaining spec — the fifteen run above cover every spec found (by grepping the whole suite for the Users/Groups sections' own placeholder text, section labels, and interaction points) to actually touch the changed surface.

Files changed: `backend/app/routers/orgs.py`, `backend/app/routers/projects.py`, `backend/tests/test_pagination.py`; `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/pages/ProjectAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/helpers.ts`, `org-group-nesting.spec.ts`, `org-security-controls.spec.ts`, `external-project-invite.spec.ts`, `project-admin-groups-and-fields.spec.ts`; `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).

## A read-only "view user" access panel

Last of the three Users/Groups roadmap items from this session — the audit's "No way to view a user's access" finding: an org admin auditing what one person can actually reach had to open every project's own membership list by hand, one at a time.

**Backend**: new `GET /orgs/{organization_id}/users/{user_id}/access` (org-admin gated), returning `{ org_groups, projects }` — `org_groups` is the user's *direct* org-group memberships; `projects` is every project in the org where `rbac.get_effective_project_roles(db, user_id, project_id)` resolves to a non-empty role set, each with that role set and which of the project's own groups the user directly belongs to. Deliberately calls the existing `get_effective_project_roles` rather than re-deriving role resolution (direct assignment, direct/nested project-group membership, org-wide-visibility grants) a second time — one algorithm serving both the actual permission checks and this read-only summary means they can't quietly drift apart. Iterates every project in the org and calls it once each, server-side, rather than exposing an N-round-trip client-side fan-out. Projects with zero access are omitted from the response entirely (this answers "what can they reach," not "here's every project with an empty role list"). New schemas `UserAccessGroupRef`/`UserAccessProject`/`UserAccessOut` in `schemas/org.py`.

**Frontend**: `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s Users table gained a per-row "View access" (eye icon) button, opening a read-only `SidePanel` — org groups as a plain list, each accessible project as a card (name linking to the project, a `.badge` per role, and a "via project group(s)" line when applicable). Fetched on demand when the panel opens, not preloaded per row.

**Verified**: New backend tests, `test_user_access.py` — a user with a direct org-group membership, a direct project role, and role-via-project-group all show up correctly; a project with zero access is excluded; non-admin gets 403; unknown user id gets 404. Full backend suite: 546/546 (unchanged from the prior entry — no other endpoint touched). `ruff check` clean. `npx tsc -b`/`npx eslint .` clean. New Storybook story `ViewUserAccessPanel` (`OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`) plus a new `getPage`-adjacent `/access` branch in the shared mock helper; full Storybook suite 55/55 files, 385/385 tests. New Playwright spec `user-access-panel.spec.ts` — views the logged-in org admin's own row (rather than depending on a second seeded persona's exact org/project membership shape) and confirms both of Alpha's projects and the "Project manager" role appear, plus Escape-to-close; re-ran alongside the six specs from the prior entry plus five more admin-surface specs, all pass. One process note worth recording since it cost real time: running `pytest` a second time mid-session (for these new tests) truncated the e2e dataset again, same as the first pagination pass — the `docker compose restart backend` + reseed step is required after *every* pytest run against `tests/container`, not just the first, and was momentarily missed here before Playwright verification caught it via a login failure.

Files changed: `backend/app/routers/orgs.py`, `backend/app/schemas/org.py`, `backend/tests/test_user_access.py`; `frontend/src/pages/OrgAdminPage.tsx` (+ `.stories.tsx`), `frontend/src/api/types.ts`, `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts`; new `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/user-access-panel.spec.ts`; `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).

## PR #10 CI hit the same apt-mirror stall as PR #9, same day — removed the `apt-get install` step entirely instead of just timing it out faster

The 5-minute `timeout-minutes` added after the PR #9 incident (previous entry) did its job — [run 32224889835](https://github.com/ballardr/reqtrackmanager/actions/runs/32224889835)'s `frontend` job failed its "Install Playwright browsers" step at exactly 5 minutes instead of hanging — but the underlying `archive.ubuntu.com`/`security.ubuntu.com` mirror stall recurred, a second time in one day, and cascaded into a spurious "Frontend coverage summary" failure (`coverage/coverage-summary.json` never got written, since the step that writes it never ran). Root-caused from the job log (`gh api repos/.../actions/jobs/<id>/logs`), same signature as before: `npm ci`/lint/build/`apt-get update` all completed normally, then nothing until `##[error]The action 'Install Playwright browsers' has timed out after 5 minutes.`

**Decided against**: leaving the timeout as the only mitigation (fails faster, but doesn't stop it recurring) or lowering/raising the timeout value (doesn't address the actual cause — a flaky Canonical mirror GitHub's own runner images depend on, entirely outside this repo's control).

**Fix**: both jobs' `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` step (the one that runs `apt-get install` for Chromium's OS deps and is what actually stalls — installing the browser binary itself is fast and was never the problem) is gone. Confirmed via the MCR registry API (`curl https://mcr.microsoft.com/v2/playwright/tags/list`) that `v1.62.1-noble` exists and matches both `frontend/package-lock.json` and `tests/playwright/package-lock.json`'s exactly-resolved `playwright`/`@playwright/test` version (`1.62.1` in both, despite `tests/playwright/package.json` still declaring `^1.47.2` — a valid range, not a bug, npm just resolved to the newest version satisfying it).

- `frontend` job now runs entirely inside `container: mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.62.1-noble` — Chromium and its OS deps are already in the image, so the "Cache Playwright browsers" and "Install Playwright browsers" steps are deleted outright, not just sped up. `actions/setup-node@v7` and every other existing action still runs fine inside a job container (the GitHub Actions runner mounts what JS-based actions need into the container automatically); no other step changed.
- `backend-and-e2e` can't take the same whole-job-container approach — it spends most of its steps running `docker compose`/`docker compose exec` directly against the runner's own Docker daemon to drive the dev/test stack, and putting that inside a job container would mean Docker-in-Docker, a materially bigger and riskier change than this incident warrants. Instead only the Playwright test run itself moves into a throwaway `docker run --network host mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.62.1-noble` container against that same host daemon — `--network host` reaches the compose stack's services on `localhost` exactly like the native process did (every service in `tests/container/docker-compose.yml` publishes a host port; `playwright.config.ts`'s `baseURL` is `http://localhost:3000`), and the working directory (already `npm ci`'d on the host in a separate step first) is bind-mounted in, so it's the same test files/config/`node_modules` as before — `@playwright/test` has no native/compiled dependencies, so a host-installed `node_modules` mounted into another Linux/glibc container is safe.
- Both `container:`/image references carry a comment that the tag's version must be bumped in lockstep with any future `playwright`/`@playwright/test` npm version bump (same lockstep discipline CLAUDE.md already requires for Node's version across `ci.yml`/`Dockerfile`/`.nvmrc`) — a mismatch would break browser launches with a clear version-mismatch error rather than silently misbehaving, but there's no automated check for it yet.

**Verified**: `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml'))"` clean. Tag existence and exact version match confirmed against the live MCR registry API and both lockfiles, as above. **Not verified end-to-end against a live GitHub Actions run** — no way to reproduce the apt-mirror stall on demand, and this needs a real PR run to confirm both `container:` jobs actually execute (in particular: `actions/setup-node` behavior inside the frontend job's container, and file ownership of report files written by `docker run`'s default-root process and then read back by `dorny/test-reporter`/`actions/upload-artifact` on the host in `backend-and-e2e` — root-owned bind-mounted files are normally world-readable so this is expected to be fine, but untested here). Flagging this explicitly rather than claiming it's proven: watch the next PR #10 CI run closely and adjust if either job surfaces a problem this reasoning didn't anticipate.

Files changed: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/decisions.md` (this entry).
